The whole token half of `router.ts` loses its callers: `verifyJwtGuard`, its `sub`-keyed limiter and the three `forwardWith…` steps. That limiter is the one thing the routes bought, and it goes with them rather than moving (the facade's rate-limit row below). `nebula-registry-scope-in-url.md`, the on-hold file that planned to put a scope on those URLs, loses its subject and is deleted — its three disk facts mined into § *Context and current state* first. |
| **Merging two Profiles happens on the Profile page, and Home only shows the link when it senses two** (Larry, 2026-09-19) | **A merge screen on the platform host** — a Profile is a mesh node, so a page there holds no way to edit one, and the work would be built where it cannot be done. Home is where a second Profile becomes visible, which makes it the place to offer the link rather than the place to run it. |
| **`lmz.broadcast` starts a fresh chain by default and `{ newChain: false }` opts out; a directed `lmz.call` inherits** (Larry, 2026-09-19 and 2026-09-28) | **Stripping `claims` from every client-bound `originAuth`** — a client's `@mesh()` methods are guardable like any node's, and a guard authorizes an incoming call from those claims, so a blanket strip takes a capability from every app we ship. **Leaving fanouts inheriting** — the reason on record is the fence itself, in `deliverPreviewReady`'s JSDoc, so it expires with it; `broadcast.ts`'s JSDoc gave the same reason until 2026-09-27. **An override on `NebulaClientGateway`'s outbound leg** — the defect is generic to any multi-tenant mesh app, so confining the fix to Nebula hides it from the package that has it. |
| **The preview-ready nudge stays point-to-point** (Larry, 2026-09-19) | **A subscription to the built digest** — `report.bundle.indexSha256` is already in hand where the announce happens, the data plane already carries enrollment and reaping, and a resubscribe would heal the reconnect gap an event cannot. It also reloads every teammate watching the galaxy the moment any build lands, and getting today's behaviour back means a client comparing digests and offering a reload rather than taking one. The nudge is wanted for whoever asked and nobody else, so the cheaper mechanism is the right one. |
| **The universe page keeps its list of the account's apps, read through the facade with its parent taken from the host's scope** (Larry, 2026-09-19). `expandScope` therefore lands in this build for that page; what stays deferred is Home calling it past its 50-node budget. | **A create form and no list** — the rows and their read both exist today, so this deletes working UI and rebuilds it for the per-app manager the page's own header promises. It also loses the behaviour that opens the create form exactly when an account is empty: with no list the page cannot tell an empty account from a full one, so someone adding their second app meets a modal over their apps. The rule it appears to fight already allows it — Home is the only list a person picks *from*, and a parent's page may list its children to manage them. |
| **`/gateway/` accepts only the client Gateway's binding: `routeDORequest` gains an opt-in `bindings` allow-list, and an unlisted binding answers `undefined`, exactly as a binding the Worker does not hold** (endpoint audit 2026-09-17; Larry, 2026-09-19 and 2026-10-01). It is checked against the normalized binding name, before the missing-instance throw, CORS and any hook, so no stub is made and nothing says which bindings exist. Nebula passes `['NEBULA_CLIENT_GATEWAY']`. | **Leaving it** — an upgrade naming `STAR` or `NEBULA_AUTH_REGISTRY` reaches no data, since a DO `fetch` answers 405 or 501 everywhere but the Galaxy's public app, but each one runs a constructor and `onStart`, leaving a billable instance with migration markers written, and the Registry's instance name is a constant, so it is an unmetered path to the singleton ([ADR-018](../docs/adr/018-singleton-is-the-scarce-resource.md)). **Dropping the binding from the path, so `/gateway/{instance}` can name nothing else** — it deletes the class rather than guarding it, but it rewrites the URL every mesh client builds and its docs, and leaves the same hole open for every other `routeDORequest` consumer, which one additive field closes for all of them. **`bindingName` on the hook context, checked in Nebula's `onBeforeConnect`** (2026-09-19, reversed 2026-10-01) — a guard every consumer has to remember to write, where the router can refuse before any hook runs. **Making the allow-list required** — it breaks `routeDORequest`'s other users. **Dropping `routeDORequest` for a fixed route** — it would reimplement the upgrade's `Origin` check, the header translation, name-versus-id handling and the hooks inside Nebula. **Throwing on an unlisted binding** — the prefix is optional, so "not mine" is `undefined`. |
| **The refresh keeps its Registry fallback on a KV miss, unguarded, and the KV record is written by the Worker the person is talking to** (Larry, 2026-09-19 and 2026-09-20) | **A signed freshness proof in the cookie**, and **a cap of one Registry read per request** — both were priced against a forged-cookie flood that cannot happen: a candidate must be named at or above the host's scope, so the scope tree caps the set at four however many cookies arrive. The proof would also have changed the value format of the highest-stakes credential we hold, reaching every `hashString` site and the `refresh:{tokenHash}` key, and silently amended ADR-022 § *The cookie rules*. **Dropping the fallback and letting the client retry** — a KV read that misses is cached at that colo like any other result, so a retry re-reads the miss rather than the store; the person would wait out the cache TTL or log in again, and a backoff loop would sit on the path every page load runs through. ⚠️ That caching behaviour is documented rather than measured here; a deployed two-colo probe settles it if the question returns. **Leaving the KV write in the Registry** — the record would keep being written at one colo and read at another, which is the gap the fallback exists for. **Resting on "a miss is rare" alone** — true of the propagation gap the Worker-side write closes, false of a revoked record, whose cookie outlives it by up to 30 days and misses on every refresh until it expires. |
| **Each Worker-side refresh-record put is followed by a check that its index row still stands, and the record is deleted if it does not** (Larry, 2026-10-01): write, then reap if orphaned, for the consume, acceptance and the fallback's self-heal. | **Reaping at the refresh** — the refresh reads nothing but Workers KV on a hit, so telling an orphan from a healthy record would put a Registry read on every refresh. **A scheduled sweep listing `refresh:` keys against the index** — its cost grows with every live session rather than with logins, against the singleton's index; held in reserve should orphans ever show in the logs. **Moving the put back into the Registry** — it narrows the gap to one KV round trip and brings back the propagation-gap miss the Worker-side write closed. **Accepting the gap** — an offboarding hole of up to 30 days, aimable by anyone who keeps their link. |
| **A person may own at most `MAX_GALAXIES_PER_OWNER` galaxies, counted live in the Registry at `createGalaxy` and at a claim's acceptance** (Larry, 2026-09-20 and 2026-10-01). It is a runaway stop, not an anti-abuse guard: more addresses defeat any per-identity cap, and what raises the bar for an adversary is the proved mailbox at the consume. What this stops is a script in a loop, an over-enthusiastic user or a bug in our own create path draining a budget every customer on the zone shares. The count is the address's ACCEPTED admin memberships, leaving out one at `_platform`, joined to a `Scopes` prefix scan per admin scope — no stored counter, no new column, and both `createGalaxy` and acceptance already run inside the Registry, so the check is local to the invocation that writes, and the hook orders only when that create or acceptance succeeds. A claim nobody accepted counts for nothing, and spends nothing, since a claim's pack is ordered at consent; a superuser is capped by their universe and galaxy memberships like anyone else. It counts galaxies others created under a universe you admin, which is your footprint on the shared zone, so the number wants headroom. Escape until Beta: ask, and we raise the constant or add a zone; at Beta, a domain of their own. It is exercised locally at its real value: a local create orders no pack and costs a few Registry rows, so the constant needs no per-run override. | **A cap per universe** — keyed on an axis that does not bound the spending: `claimUniverse` applies no per-address bound and Turnstile is OFF in production (backlog.md's opening banner), so one mailbox claims universes without limit, each claim writes its first galaxy, and a fresh universe has zero galaxies — so a per-universe cap never fires on the runaway path while refusing a single-universe customer their 21st app. **The check in the facade** — counting in the Worker and writing in the Durable Object is check-then-act across a hop, and it would cover `createGalaxy` while missing `claim-universe`, which enters over HTTP through `forwardRaw` and is the runaway path. **A counter on the Profile DO** — a denormalized copy owing convergence, for a low-frequency event whose write already happens where the data is. **Counting every membership the address holds** (2026-09-20, reversed 2026-10-01) — a claim names any address and is never reaped, so claims naming a stranger would fill their count and refuse their own signup for good; and a superuser's root membership would count every galaxy on the zone. **Counting claims whose link was consumed** — a per-membership stamp, a new column, to keep ordering at the consume. **No cap at all** — defensible while every user is someone we know, but the consequence of being wrong is an outage for every customer rather than a degraded experience for one. |
| **The claim writes the universe, its first galaxy and that galaxy's `.dev` Star in one act, and this build owns it** (Larry, 2026-09-20) | **Leaving the whole step to the shared-pages item** — that item has no task file and no ordering against this build, so two criteria here could not pass from this build alone, and § *Certificates and the wait*'s walk would be conditional on a file nobody has written: the pack is ordered at consent, and what it orders is the galaxy the claim created, so without the galaxy there is nothing to order and the signup walk changes shape. **A claim that writes only the universe, with the galaxy created afterwards from the universe page** — that is the flow § *Signing up lands in Studio* replaced, which made a new user learn universe, galaxy and Star before reaching Studio. |
| **`claim-star` refuses a galaxy whose universe nobody has accepted** (Larry, 2026-10-01). From Phase 8 a claim writes its galaxy before anyone accepts it, so without this a stranger could claim and accept a tenant beneath it and keep minting an admin token there for 30 days after the claim is superseded. It is the integrity check `claimStar` already makes, that the parent exists, applied to a parent nobody has taken up, and it approves nothing, so open self-signup is untouched. | **Cleaning up at convergence** — revoking every session in the retired subtree, running its teardown and recording it, three pieces of machinery guarding a state the refusal makes impossible (`calibration.md` §2). **Recording the residual** — the stranger would hold an admin session inside someone else's future app. |
| **`PLATFORM_SCOPE` becomes `'_platform'`, not `'platform'`** (Larry, 2026-09-21) | **`'platform'`** — it spells the same as the host label in `platform.lumenize.dev`, so the bare word stops saying which of the two a sentence means, and those two are opposites: the root scope is what dominion comes from, while the platform host is the one host that hands out no token. Four things follow from the underscore, and none of them costs anything, since renaming a stored scope id rides the wipe either way. It cannot be a slug — `SLUG_RE` requires `[a-z0-9]` first — so it cannot be a host label either, which makes the distinction structural rather than a note someone has to remember. It deletes a guard: `claimUniverse` currently refuses `PLATFORM_SCOPE` by name because today's value IS a claimable slug, and `claimUniverseWithTicket` carries its own copy; the validator now refuses it first, and both copies go. It coins nothing — `SCOPELESS_INSTANCE_TAG` is `'_scopeless'` for this exact reason, stated in its JSDoc. And a cookie named `__Host-refresh-token._platform` says scope on its face. ⚠️ `parseId` admits it where the root flows through, since `isPlatformScope` is a plain equality check and validation order decides. |
| **Each facade method refuses on the verified claims before calling the Registry, and the Registry keeps its own check** (2026-09-29, the early panel) | **Leaving the refusal in the Registry alone, as today's routes do** — every refused create and delete would wake the singleton, which `issueInvites`' JSDoc already names as the reason its eligibility is the caller's job. **Dropping the Registry's check once the facade has one** — a path that reaches the Registry another way would meet nothing; the in-method check is the invariant. |
| **nebula-auth reaches the platform's Durable Objects through `ScopeLifecycleHooks` — `orderCertificate` and `teardown` — and `apps/nebula` answers each with a raw RPC call to a method decorated `@rawRpc()`, through `rawRpcStub`** (Larry, 2026-10-01). Each teardown rides the record of the act that called for it, since the call carries no principal: the deletion's, `createGalaxy`'s, or the acceptance's (ADR-016). | **A wake from the universe page, as the file first said** — a mesh call creates a Durable Object for any name, so a universe admin could wake `acme.g1`, `acme.g2`, … and order a pack for each past the owner cap, and consent holds no token to make the call at all. **Admitting claim-free calls from `NEBULA_AUTH_FACADE` in `NebulaDO.onBeforeCall`** — a trust rule on every node to serve one wake, and consent still has no claims to start from. **A raw `fetch` to `POST /_order-certificate` and `POST /_teardown`, with the identity headers** (Larry, 2026-10-01, reversed the same day) — it put operations only our own code may invoke on a Durable Object's HTTP surface, which ADR-007 keeps for page traffic and the node's own container, guarded only by the page step forwarding `GET` alone (§ *What reaches a Durable Object's `fetch`*). **Event-named hooks, `onGalaxyCreated` and `onScopesDeleted`** — a claim writes its galaxy at claim time, so the first would be false on the claim path. **A claim-free `lmz.call` path past `onBeforeCall`** — it would open every `@mesh()` method to a chain carrying no claims, and the broadcast tier, removed 2026-09-27, and `mesh-calls-to-and-from-clients.md`'s rejected synthetic `originAuth` both turned down that lane. The entry adopted instead invokes only a method `@rawRpc()` decorated, by its one name, so it reaches nothing a raw RPC call to that method would not. **Defining the hooks on `apps/nebula`'s facade subclass** — every call back into the mesh would then sit inside a class named for the bridge going the other way. **Keeping the browser's teardown fan-out** — it lived in the Manage panel, which this build deletes, and a page closed mid-delete leaves a scope's Durable Objects behind for whoever re-claims its slug. |
| **A scope's Durable Objects are torn down when it is created as well as when it is deleted** (Larry, 2026-10-01). A creation happens only where no `Scopes` row exists, so whatever the new scope's objects hold is leftover, whether a failed teardown left it or a live token wrote it after one. What a creation does not write, a deleted account's other apps, is a recorded residual that a periodic sweep removes (Larry, 2026-10-02). | **A Registry tombstone refusing claims of a deleted slug until every teardown confirms, with failed targets retried from an alarm** — state and an alarm on the singleton (ADR-018), and it misses an object a live token recreated after its teardown, which still confirmed. **Recording the residual for the scopes a creation writes** — the second path is the ordinary case of a teammate with a tab open, not a failure. **A created marker each node checks before answering, written by the creation's teardown** (2026-10-02) — one KV read on every mesh call, and every fixture that addresses a node directly marked first, against a residual a sweep removes. **Reusing the identity stamp as that marker** — whoever touches a node first writes it, by design, which is the caller the marker would have to refuse. |
| **Our own code calls a mesh node by raw RPC through `rawRpcStub`, and only methods decorated `@rawRpc()` answer** (Larry, 2026-10-01; [ADR-023](../docs/adr/023-the-mesh-boundary-is-crossed-at-a-bridge.md)). The stub is typed from the node's class and sends each call to one entry, which stamps identity, checks the decorator and invokes the one method, so stamping lives in one place and every call site is typed. `@rawRpc()` names the path a method may be called by, as `@mesh()` does, in the word the repo already uses for it. | **A decorator that takes the binding and instance names as the method's first two arguments** — a decorator cannot change the signature callers see, so each call site would need a cast, and an undecorated method called that way would run with two extra arguments. **An entry taking the method's name as a string at every call site** — untyped, with no opt-in. **`@internal()` or `@trusted()`** — neither names the path, and `@trusted()` reads as safe when it means the method trusts its caller completely. **`@raw()`** — raw what. |
| **What reaches a Durable Object's `fetch` is laid as three tracks: page traffic only under `/_public/`, our own code by raw RPC, a node's own container at its library's fixed paths** (Larry, 2026-10-01). ADR-007 holds the commitment, `raw-comm.md` the mechanics, `mesh.md` `@rawRpc()` and `rawRpcStub`, and `npm run audit:do-http` the check. | **Guarding each internal route by method** — a forward that changes which paths it produces opens a route nobody meant to expose, as the page step's path-preserving forward would have done to the container's path. **`/_internal/` routes beside `/_public/`** — also structural, but it keeps operations only our own code may invoke in the namespace pages reach. **An ADR of its own** — ADR-007 already commits to it, and only its enumeration of today's surfaces needed restating. **Requiring the `bindings` allow-list in `routeDORequest`** — it breaks the router's other users. |
| **A host the parse refuses gets a 404 at page dispatch, a 403 with no CORS headers at the refresh, and a 400 at the login** (2026-09-29, the early panel) | **One status for all three** — a person loading a page wants a page, and the refresh's answer should be unreadable to the script that asked. **Letting the parse throw** — `router.ts`'s blanket 500 hides which caller refused, which is the defect today's body parse was written to avoid. |
| **`return_to` keeps neither the hour cap nor the read-once that `localStorage` gave it** (2026-09-29, the early panel) | **Carrying both onto the magic-link record** — the record's 30-minute TTL already bounds it, and a link is spent when it is used, so it lands on its page once, which is what read-once protected against when the destination sat in the browser. |
| **The owner cap is exercised locally at its real value** (2026-09-29, the early panel) | **A `wrangler.jsonc` var the harness lowers through `bootVars`** — a local create orders no pack, so reaching the cap costs one facade call per galaxy, and a var would add a production setting whose only reader is a test (`calibration.md` §3(d)). **Exercising it on the deployed target** — every galaxy there orders a real pack from the pool the cap protects. |
| **The Galaxy's certificate ordering is a pure state machine tested in-lane; its wiring to the hook is verified locally by marker, and its calls to Cloudflare's API only on the deployed target** (2026-09-29, the early panel) | **Forcing a transient failure in `/live`** — nothing makes ACM fail on a real zone without a helper that bridges the difference, which `live.md` forbids. **Mocking the Cloudflare API under pool-workers** — a mock of the one API whose behaviour is in question asserts only what the mock was told. |
| **`deploy-test.sh` rewrites every `services[].service` to the worker name it deploys** (2026-09-29, the early panel) | **Relying on wrangler to retarget a self-reference** — wrangler 4.124 rewrites nothing, and [backlog.md](backlog.md) already records the same hazard measured for Worker Previews: a self-referencing binding resolves to production, silently. |
| **A persona's `sub` and `profileId` are one version-5 UUID, computed from a fixed Lumenize namespace UUID and the persona's name in its Star, `acme.crm.dev/manny`** (Larry, 2026-09-29). It is a GUID wherever it travels, and deterministic, so the refresh derives it from the host and nothing stores it. A person's ids come from `crypto.randomUUID()`, which always writes 4 as the version digit, so the persona path can never mint a real person's token — by construction, not by odds. One value serves both, as `agent:nebula` does, since a persona has no memberships for a separate `sub` to tell apart. | **A random GUID** — it must be stored where the refresh can read it, which brings back the persona record, and the wait before a first tab, that deriving from the host removed on 2026-09-18. **A readable `persona:acme:crm:dev:manny`** — the readability buys little, and it was shaped around the Gateway splitting a client id at its first `.`, which is the tail wagging the dog: a `tabId` never holds a dot, so splitting at the last one is a one-line change if a `sub` ever needs one. **The scope plus a GUID in the `sub`, with a GUID `profileId`** — the same small readability gain, for two spellings. **A separate `profileId` carrying the scope** — a Profile belongs to no scope. |
| **The claim takes the account's and first app's slugs only, and the universe build adds their names** (Larry, 2026-09-29), in the change that stores them as scope-metadata resources on their hosts. Nothing renders a name before either build lands, so leaving them out here costs nothing a user sees. | **Writing the names on `Scopes` now, as [nebula-pre-alpha.md](nebula-pre-alpha.md) § *Scope full names* says** — the universe build would then move them, so a column, its writes and its tests are built to be unbuilt, the interim that file warns about. **Taking the names in the claim and storing nothing yet** — a parameter that silently does nothing until a later build wires it. |
| **No mesh call is rate limited, the facade's included, until a looping caller is a real problem** (Larry, 2026-09-29, raised by the early panel). `NEBULA_AUTH_RATE_LIMITER` goes with the routes that called it. Every mesh caller holds a token naming them, so a caller in a loop can be found and cut off, and what it costs is Registry load, which is recoverable: the list read and impersonation only read, and deletion runs only where the caller holds dominion already. The one method that spends something shared and scarce, creating a galaxy, is capped per owner. | **The facade checking the `sub`-keyed limiter before its hop** — one check in the one place, but it builds for a problem nobody has had, and the cap already bounds the one path that spends a scarce resource. **A per-`sub` limit at the Gateway on every mesh call** — the right shape once the problem arrives, since the Gateway sees every call's verified `sub`, and a backlog row records it; before then it is a new mechanism in `@lumenize/mesh` that would throttle ordinary Star traffic too. **Keeping the binding unused** — a limiter nothing calls reads as a protection that exists. |
| **An ADR-016 record carries `aud`** (2026-09-29, the early panel; ADR-016 amended at Pass 2) | **Recording `access` alone, as today** — under the host rule it no longer answers "how was this permitted?", the question ADR-016's own rejected alternative names. `aud` stays out of the coalesce key and off the wire, as `access` does. |

## Phases

Numbered in the order they are built. Every rule below binds every phase, so no phase repeats it.

- **Every instrument is RUN before it is written down, and what it returned is recorded at the phase.** A `grep` nobody executed is prose. Each count quoted below carries the date it was run, and a phase re-runs it rather than trusting it.
- **An enumeration is derived, never frozen.** Name the instrument and the PROPERTY every member must satisfy, and record the count at the phase.
- **Each criterion names its fixture, its mutation and a positive control, per limb** (`live.md` § *A multi-limb scenario*), and matches a refusal by its MESSAGE, never as a bare boolean.
- **`/live` is the default tier** (`live.md`). A criterion drops to pool-workers for a parser, a predicate or a pure state machine, or where the behaviour is genuinely unreachable through the running system — a timing window no scenario can hit, a call only our own code can make (`testing.md`) — and says which.
- **Each phase deletes the "Today's code differs" clauses it makes false**, in ADR-007, 015, 021, 022 and 023 and in `docs/vision/auth.md`, in the same commit. A clause no phase closes names the file that will.
- **A rule, skill or published doc describing today's code changes in the last phase that changes what it describes**, never earlier (§ *Constraints*, the two waves), judged clause by clause rather than file by file. Each phase names the clauses it owns.
- **A limb that counts log lines counts only the lines its own request caused.** wrangler prints a request's access-log line after the Worker's own output, and a facade call over the socket prints none, so a slice between access-log lines holds nothing it means to count. Every route under `/auth/` and every facade method therefore takes a correlation id at entry, the Worker's request id or the facade's mesh call id, logs it on a named entry line, stamps it on every marker, record and teardown line it causes, and logs a completion line after its hooks return. A limb filters by that id, with `live.md`'s presence guard.
- **Nothing is compensated for.** A helper under `apps/nebula/harness/` performs only steps production performs (`live.md` § *A `/live` scenario MUST NOT compensate for its environment*).
- **Every lane is green at every commit, or the phase names what it leaves red and which phase greens it.** That means `npm test` in `apps/nebula` and `packages/nebula-auth`, its `browser` lane included, the `chromium` and `ui-smoke` lanes, `npm run test:doc`'s checker, and `drive.ts all` (`live.md`). A `@check-example` block whose checked source a phase edits is conformed in that phase.
- **No task-file handle ships in source.** A phase's changed files pass `workflow.md`'s grep for `S`/`D` handles and `Phase N`, read hit by hit, so a skip reason or a comment cites the host rule, an ADR or a rule file instead.

1. **Every scope name is a legal host label, and the root cannot be one.** `PLATFORM_SCOPE` becomes `'_platform'`, and every copy of the old literal changes with it: `git grep -n 'nebula-platform' -- . ':!**/archive/**' ':!experiments/**'` returned 119 lines in 45 files on 2026-09-30, and returns only prose that names the rename afterwards, plus the dated records it leaves alone: `apps/nebula/harness/FINDINGS.md` and `tasks/backlog.md`'s rows. `RESERVED_STAR_SLUGS` holds ADR-021's eight environment names, and `test-helpers.ts`'s copy folds into the import. `RESERVED_UNIVERSE_SLUGS` gains `platform`, `email` and `www` and keeps the labels it holds today, its JSDoc re-deriving each as a name kept for a platform host rather than a path segment. Both claim paths call one predicate for it, where today only `claimUniverse` checks the set. `isValidSlug` caps a slug at 30 characters, and `validateSlug` in `org-ops.ts` calls it.
   - **The root still parses where it flows through.** `parseId` admits it before the slug grammar, which refuses it everywhere else, so the three parses that still read it — `parseScopeGuard` in `router.ts`, and the refresh's and the mint's body parse in `worker-token.ts` — keep working until Phases 7 and 10 delete them (§ *Decisions*, the `_platform` row).
   - **Fixtures fit the cap.** The three `uniqueGalaxyScope` helpers in `apps/nebula/test/test-apps/dev-studio/`, which spell a universe slug from a whole UUID, take a shorter random slug, since the cap refuses 36 characters.
   - **Success criteria (capable of failing):**
     - *The root cannot be claimed, and no guard names it.* A claim naming `PLATFORM_SCOPE` itself — the constant, not a literal, so the mutation below moves the target with it — is refused by slug validation, matched by its message, and `claimUniverse`'s and `claimUniverseWithTicket`'s by-name checks are gone. Mutation: set `PLATFORM_SCOPE` to a value `SLUG_RE` accepts and the reserved host-label set does not catch — `root`, say, since `platform` *is* caught — and the claim is admitted with no check standing, minting a `scopeAdmin` membership at the root. Positive control: a claim for `acme` succeeds.
     - *Both claim paths refuse a host label.* A signup-ticket claim for `www` is refused with the reserved-slug message, as a direct claim is. Mutation: drop the predicate from `claimUniverseWithTicket`, and the ticket path claims `www` — which it can do today for `app` or `auth`.
     - *A slug caps at 30 characters.* `northwind-traders-international`, at 31, is refused at both claim paths and as an org-tree node slug; `warehouse-management-system`, at 27, passes. Mutation: drop the cap, and the 31-character claim succeeds.
     - *No node named by an id parses as a scope.* `parseId` refuses a `profileId`, a persona's version-5 id and a Gateway's `{sub}.{tabId}`, each of which it accepts today because a UUID passes the slug grammar. `mesh-calls-to-and-from-clients.md`'s D10 decides passage from a node's name only when that name parses, so this is what keeps the Profile from reading as a universe. Mutation: drop the cap, and all three parse.
     - *An environment name is not a Star slug.* `claim-star` for `staging` under a galaxy is refused. Mutation: restore `{'dev'}` alone.
     - *The root still works where it flows through.* `superuser-front-door` passes: the bootstrap address logs in, accepts and refreshes at `_platform`, and holds passage into a universe. Mutation: let `parseId` refuse the root, and the platform refresh answers 400.
     - *Every suite passes under the cap.* `npm test` in `apps/nebula` and `packages/nebula-auth` is green, and `drive.ts all` sweeps green, since a slug the cap refuses throws wherever a fixture builds one.
   - **Guidance owned:** `.claude/skills/live/SKILL.md`'s `nebula-platform` mention, since no later phase renames the root. ADR-021's "Today's code differs" clauses on the platform name, the length cap and the reserved sets, leaving the persona floor. The reservation clauses in ADR-015 and `auth.md` § *Coarse-grained access control* respell the root as `_platform`; they survive this build, since the reserved name keeps a refusal of its own.

2. **A push speaks for the node, not the writer** (§ *A push speaks for the node, not the writer*). `lmz.broadcast` starts a fresh chain by default, and `{ newChain: false }` opts back into inheritance. The Profile's explicit `newChain: true` goes. `deliverPreviewReady` passes `newChain: true` and stays point-to-point. The Gateway's `aud` fence and its Profile exemption are replaced in the same phase, since the fence refuses a push whose claims are absent: `NebulaClientGateway.onBeforeCallToClient` checks the tab's passage into the sender's scope instead (§ *A push speaks for the node, not the writer*).
   - **Who the check treats as the sender.** A node, whose scope is its name when that parses as one, or another Client, whose scope is the `aud` its own Gateway verified. A node whose name parses as no scope, the Profile, passes, and an envelope that names no sender at all is refused.
   - **What a refusal says and leaves.** It names passage and both scopes, in wording coined here, which Phase 12 gives `requirePassage` too. A subscriber row the check refuses stays in place until its tab disconnects, since the refusal reaches the sender untyped and the reapers drop a row only on `ClientDisconnectedError`; such a refusal is rare, and the row's tab still exists.
   - **Success criteria (capable of failing):**
     - *The default flips in `@lumenize/mesh`.* The default case in `packages/mesh/test/broadcast.test.ts` inverts: a broadcast with no chain option reaches each target with only the broadcaster in `callChain` and no `originAuth`. Mutation: restore inheritance, and it reds. A second limb: `{ newChain: false }` reaches each target with the caller's chain and `originAuth`. Mutation: ignore the option, and it reds.
     - *The fence is gone.* `grep -nE "=== 'PROFILE'|aud !== " apps/nebula/src/nebula-client-gateway.ts` returned 2 lines on 2026-09-27 and returns nothing. `profile-subscribe.test.ts`'s *the fence is PROFILE-scoped* test is deleted, and the aud-fence tests are rewritten for the passage check, in `client-gateway-passage.test.ts`, except *REFUSES sibling-tenant delivery for ONE admin whose scope covers both*: until Phase 12 passage reads `authScope`, so that admin passes by dominion. That case is rewritten to its target shape — sender `acme.crm.a` in `callChain`, a galaxy admin's tab at `aud` `acme.crm.b`, the passage message — confirmed to red for that reason, then `it.skip`ped with a reason citing the host rule (`testing.md` § *Deferring ≠ deleting*), so Phase 12 only un-skips it.
     - *Passage decides what reaches a tab.* In-lane, since a Star calling a tab is a call only our own code can make (`testing.md`). The tab's holder is a plain member of its Star, since until Phase 12 a galaxy admin passes a sibling by dominion. A Galaxy's update reaches a tab on one of its Stars' pages, a call from a sibling Star to that tab is refused and matched by its message, and a Profile push and a fresh-chain update both arrive. Mutations: check the sender's passage into the tab's scope instead, and the Galaxy's update is refused; drop the check, and the sibling Star's call arrives.
     - *A Client sender is checked by the `aud` its Gateway verified.* In `/live`, a plain member's tab on `acme.crm.a` calls a tab on `acme.crm.b` directly, and the receiving Gateway refuses it with its own passage message. Mutation: drop the Client branch, and the refusal that arrives is the client's own *Direct client-to-client calls are disabled by default*. Positive control: a call between two tabs on one Star passes the Gateway and meets that client refusal. A third limb, in-lane through `client-gateway-passage.test.ts`'s `envelope()` helper, since no running system sends one: `buildOutgoingCallContext` always seeds a chain, and the Gateway seeds `[verifiedOrigin]`. An envelope with an empty `callChain` is refused. Mutation: treat an absent sender as naming no scope, and it passes.
     - *No writer's claims reach any page.* `update-identity.test.ts`'s *every kind of update reaches the probe with no originAuth and a chain naming only the host* is un-skipped and green. Mutations: pass `newChain: false` at the plane's one `#send`, and it reds; send any one kind with a plain `lmz.call`, and its offender list names that kind. Positive control: the Profile's initial snapshot to its own subscriber still carries that subscriber's claims, since a snapshot answers the subscriber's own call (§ *A push speaks for the node, not the writer*), which `profile-subscribe.test.ts` keeps as proof the probe records `originAuth` at all.
     - *A push carries no claims, and a directed call does.* A subscriber's `@mesh()` handler and the raw `incoming_call` frame on its socket both show no `originAuth` for a fanout, while a directed call down to a client carries one. Mutation: restore the inherit default, and the writer's claims are back in the frame.
     - *Only the client that asked for a build has its preview reloaded.* Two Studio clients on one galaxy; the one that asked sees the nudge, and the other does not. Mutations: fan the nudge out, and the second client reloads too; drop `newChain` from the nudge, and its frame carries the asking client's claims.
     - *Delivery survives.* `profile-subscribe.test.ts`'s HEADLINE delivery assertion and its disconnected-row drop, and `profile-do.test.ts`'s reaper test, stay green. HEADLINE's fence wording and its fence mutation retire with the fence.
     - *No comment claims the fence holds.* `grep -rnE "aud[^a-z]{0,3}(fence|lock|check)|same-aud|aud-lock|outbound fence|locks the active scope" apps packages --include='*.ts' | grep -v node_modules | grep -v platform-embed | grep -v /dist/` returned 21 lines in 13 files on 2026-09-30, and returns only lines that describe something other than the fence, each read: `peer-guard.test.ts`'s, which says the mesh's own hook is a no-op; `scope-verification.test.ts`'s, which describes verification's own at-or-below check; `scope-isolation.test.ts`'s history of the trust-on-first-use aud-lock; and its line 232, whose "aud check" Phase 12 makes true again. Mutation: leave `nebula-do.ts`'s or `verify.ts`'s comment, and the sweep returns it.
   - **Guidance owned:** `website/docs/mesh/broadcast.mdx`, which states the inherit default twice, and the comment in `packages/mesh/test/for-docs/calls/document-do.ts` that cites a deleted Nebula `broadcast.ts`. ADR-022's and `auth.md`'s fence clauses, and ADR-015's clause that no Gateway checks passage on a call to a client; `auth.md` § *How the claims travel* already describes the passage check, so only the fence sentences of the note under § *`activeScope`* go; that note's "passage and dominion still read `authScope`" is Phase 12's. `packages/mesh/src/broadcast.ts`'s JSDoc, which states the inherit default and cites this task file by path, which source may not (`workflow.md`). `website/docs/mesh/gateway.mdx`'s multi-tenant example, which stamps a `tenantId` in the call context and refuses a mismatch in `onBeforeCallToClient`, so a fresh-chain broadcast would be refused; it is rewritten to check the connection's right to receive from `callChain.at(-1)`, and its `@skip-check` block is why no lane catches it.

3. **`/gateway/` accepts one binding, refused before any Durable Object exists.** `@lumenize/routing`'s `routeDORequest` gains an opt-in `bindings` allow-list. It is checked against the binding name the router resolved, before the missing-instance throw, CORS and any hook, and an unlisted binding answers `undefined`, exactly as a binding the Worker does not hold. Nebula passes `['NEBULA_CLIENT_GATEWAY']` (§ *Decisions*).
   - **Success criteria (capable of failing):**
     - *Another binding is refused at the door.* In `/live`, after a real login, a Node WebSocket upgrade to `/gateway/STAR/whatever` carrying that token answers 404. The Star's `onStart` marker, which this phase adds as `nebula.Star.onStart` carrying `ctx.id.name`, since `onStart` runs before any identity is stamped (`profile.ts` reads it at construction for the same reason), never appears in the stack's stdio, read with the presence guard after the upgrade's own access-log line, which wrangler prints after the Worker's output (`live.md`). Mutation: drop the option, and the marker appears. Positive controls: `/gateway/NEBULA_CLIENT_GATEWAY/{sub}.{tabId}` connects, and a mesh call to that Star fires the marker.
     - *The router refuses before it resolves anything.* A `routeDORequest` unit test with `bindings: ['ALPHA_GATEWAY']` returns `undefined` for `/gateway/BETA/inst`, `/gateway//BETA/inst` and `/gateway/BETA`, and routes `/gateway/alpha-gateway/inst`, a segment the router resolves to `ALPHA_GATEWAY`. Mutations, one per limb: compare the raw segment, and `/gateway/alpha-gateway/inst` is refused; check after the missing-instance throw, and `/gateway/BETA` throws; check after CORS, and an unlisted binding's preflight gets CORS headers. Positive control: without the option, every binding routes as today.

4. **The cookie jar behaves like a browser** (§ *Venues*). `@lumenize/testing`'s jar learns four rules, each pinned in `packages/testing/test/unit/cookie-utils.test.ts` or `browser.test.ts`. This is product feedback for the package rather than a Nebula workaround, and every scenario after Phase 10 rides it.
   - **Success criteria (capable of failing), one per rule:**
     - *Host-only against `Domain`.* A cookie set without `Domain` on `crm.acme.lumenize.localhost` is not sent to `dev.crm.acme.lumenize.localhost`; one set with `Domain=lumenize.localhost` is. Mutation: default a missing domain to the hostname and match with `endsWith`, as `browser.ts` does today, and the host-only cookie leaks.
     - *Dot-boundary matching.* A cookie for `crm.acme.lumenize.localhost` is not sent to `xcrm.acme.lumenize.localhost`. Mutation: the bare `endsWith`, and it is.
     - *The `__Host-` and `__Secure-` rules.* A `__Host-` cookie carrying `Domain`, lacking `Secure`, or with a `Path` other than `/` is rejected at set time, and a `__Secure-` one lacking `Secure` is rejected. Mutation: accept a `__Host-` cookie with a `Domain`, and the jar sends it where a browser would not.
     - *`*.localhost` is a secure context.* A `Secure` cookie set over `http://platform.lumenize.localhost:8787` is kept and sent back over `http`. Mutation: exempt only literal `localhost`, and every `__Host-` cookie in the registry is withheld.
   - Positive control for all four: the existing Coach Carol path tests stay green.

5. **A Star's admin acts through the bypass, and no seed is written** (§ *Sessions*). The seed in `star.ts`, its `__nebula_rootAdminSeeded` flag, and every test and comment that leans on them go: `git grep -nE '__nebula_rootAdminSeeded|root-admin seeding|resetAndProbeRootAdmin|founder stamp|root admin grant|seeded root' -- apps packages` returned 17 lines in 8 files on 2026-09-30, read hit by hit, and returns nothing afterwards. `invite-roundtrip`'s limb 4, which asserts the seeded grant, re-grounds on the bypass or is deleted with its reason in the scenario's header; `resetAndProbeRootAdmin` goes; and `org-tree.ts`'s `evaluatePermissions` JSDoc, which says a Star admin needs no `hasDominionOverHost`, is corrected. The bypass comment in `org-tree.ts` stops calling a Star admin a DAG `admin` grant on root. `dev-star-data-lifecycle.test.ts`'s skipped reseed test goes with the seed.
   - **Success criteria (capable of failing):**
     - *A star-level `scopeAdmin` acts on its own tree with no DAG grant.* The fixture is a `.dev` admin from the 2026-08-27 co-mint, which `org-tree.test.ts` still calls unmintable; that claim is corrected. Mutation: drop the bypass's equality arm, confining it to an admin strictly above the host, and the star admin is refused, matched by message. Positive control: the same call succeeds unmutated, so the arm is not green against a bypass that refuses everyone.
   - **Guidance owned:** `auth.md`'s data-plane clause about the seed.

6. **What a user sees names neither a host nor the code name.** A picture upload answers the relative `/pictures/{key}`, and `profile-pictures.ts`'s JSDoc re-derives why the serve stays unauthenticated (§ *Serving*). Every user-visible "Nebula" reads "Lumenize" — the page titles, `LoginScreen.vue`, `App.vue`'s thinking and chat-header text, and `NebulaEmailSender.appName`, but not the signed-out landing's welcome, a screen Phase 10 deletes — found by `git grep -nw Nebula -- 'apps/nebula-studio-ui/*.vue' 'apps/nebula-studio-ui/*.html'`, which returned 12 lines on 2026-09-30, read for strings rather than comments. The agent's self-seeded Profile `name` and `nickname` in `profile.ts`, which every chat byline shows, and the Workspace's `GIT_AUTHOR` in `galaxy.ts` read "Lumenize" too.
   - **Success criteria (capable of failing):**
     - *A stored picture names no host.* An upload stores `/pictures/{key}`. Mutation: answer the absolute URL again, and the stored value names the uploading host. (Phase 10 adds the cross-host render.)
     - *Mail says Lumenize.* A real magic-link email's subject and body name Lumenize and not Nebula. Mutation: restore `appName`.
     - *The assertions on the old words move with them.* Every assertion on a string this phase renames, found by grepping the test and harness trees for each renamed string, `App.vue`'s `<h1>Nebula Studio</h1>` among them, changes with it. Most of those that check a name is absent count to zero, so one left unchanged passes while testing nothing. The signed-out landing's "Welcome to Nebula" stays, and so do the assertions on it, until Phase 10 deletes the screen. `drive.ts all` and `npm test` are green.

7. **What a session does rides the mesh.** `expandScope`, `createGalaxy`, `planScopeDeletion`, `executeScopeDeletion` and `impersonate(sub)` become `NebulaAuthFacade` methods, named for the Registry methods they call (§ *Decisions*). Each refuses on the verified claims before its hop, and the Registry keeps its own check (§ *Which host answers what*). `expandScope` takes its parent from the caller's `aud`. `impersonate` takes the child's `aud` from the caller's, and keeps `canMintFor` and the mint invariants. `create-star`, `createDevWorkspace`, `develop()`'s repair and the Registry's `#hasDominionOverGalaxy`, whose one caller is `createStar`, are deleted, and `provisionAndLogin` creates a tenant Star with `claim-star`. Every method here that creates, deletes or moves authority records through `projectActingToken`, which gains `aud`.
   - **Each method fails closed, and hands the Registry the claims.** The facade refuses a call that carries no claims once, in its `onBeforeCall`, keeping `invite`'s message, rather than in each method; and each method hands the Registry the verified claims as one argument it both checks and records, so the Registry's own check can follow the host rule at Phase 12; `executeScopeDeletion`'s JSDoc, which says its claims are recorded and never consulted, is re-derived.
   - **The token routes go but one.** `expand-scope`, `create-galaxy`, `create-star`, `delete-scope`, `delete-scope-plan` and `mint-narrower-token` go, with `forwardWithAccess`, `forwardWithClaims`, `subRateLimitGuard` and the Registry `fetch` cases behind them, since those cases trust the body's `verifiedAccess` once nothing upstream assigns it. `scope-summary` keeps `verifyJwtGuard` and `forwardWithSubject` until Phase 10, since Home still reads it, and loses its limiter here. `NEBULA_AUTH_RATE_LIMITER` goes with its last caller, its row in `router.ts`'s `UNCONFIGURED_PROTECTIONS` included, which would otherwise log an error in production on every Registry start (§ *Decisions*): `git grep -l NEBULA_AUTH_RATE_LIMITER` lists `apps/nebula/wrangler.jsonc`, three test configs, three generated `worker-configuration.d.ts` files, `packages/nebula-auth/README.md` and `router.ts` today, and returns only task files once `npm run types` has regenerated the `worker-configuration.d.ts` files.
   - **A deletion tears down its Durable Objects server-side.** `ScopeLifecycleHooks` lands with its `teardown` member, and Phase 15 adds `orderCertificate`. `apps/nebula/test/browser/worker` gains the binding and the export here: the `browser` lane's `multi-client` and `smoke` tests create galaxies, and its config binds only its email sender today. `executeScopeDeletion` calls `teardown`, and `apps/nebula` makes a raw RPC call to `teardown()` on each Durable Object the deletion names, through `rawRpcStub` and the `@rawRpc()` entry, which land here in `@lumenize/mesh` (§ *What reaches a Durable Object's `fetch`*). `NebulaDO.teardown()` loses its `@mesh()` decorator, and `App.vue`'s browser fan-out goes. The facade's `createGalaxy` calls `teardown` over the galaxy and its `.dev` Star before it answers, once the Registry has written them, and never after a refusal such as `slug_taken` (§ *Which host answers what*).
     - **The entry's pair is measured, and the result recorded here.** Whether `env[binding].idFromName(instanceName)` equals `ctx.id` only for the right pair is measured under `wrangler dev`; if it does, the entry verifies the pair it is handed rather than trusting it, and ADR-023's last Negative is rewritten to match.
     - **`hooks` is required.** A facade subclass without them does not compile, since an optional seam would skip a teardown silently; `routeNebulaAuthRequest`'s options require them from Phase 8, where the first route fires one. nebula-auth's own test worker passes a stub that records each call, which gives that lane a witness of every hook it fires.
     - **The hooks live in one module, and the facade only receives them.** `apps/nebula/src/scope-lifecycle-hooks.ts` exports `scopeLifecycleHooks`, which holds every `rawRpcStub` call `apps/nebula` makes. `apps/nebula` subclasses `NebulaAuthFacade` under the same name, so the binding's `entrypoint` is unchanged, exports it from every Worker that creates a galaxy, and has the subclass set `hooks` and nothing else (§ *Which host answers what*).
     - **The consent path's Profile calls take the same bridge.** nebula-auth reaches the `Profile` by raw RPC today for the display-name pre-fill and write; those calls move onto `rawRpcStub`, with `@rawRpc()` on `readDisplayNames` and `setDisplayNames` ([ADR-023](../docs/adr/023-the-mesh-boundary-is-crossed-at-a-bridge.md)). nebula-auth imports the stub from `@lumenize/mesh/raw-rpc`, never the root barrel, which would pull the mesh node graph into its light index. `PROFILE` becomes bound in every Worker that runs the consent route, nebula-auth's test worker and `apps/nebula/test/browser/worker` included, so the generated `Env` types the call; an absent binding stops being a configuration, and a failing call stays best-effort, logged and never refusing entry.
     - **Each target is caught on its own.** One failing call is logged at error, naming the instance it missed, and the rest still run.
     - **A wiped object resets.** `teardown()` awaits the Galaxy's build container's `destroy()` unconditionally, tolerating every failure and logging no build marker, then calls `deleteAll()`, yields a macrotask with `await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 0))`, then calls `ctx.abort('scope-deleted')`. It does not reuse `#destroyBuildContainer`, which is deliberately not awaited, nor gate on `running`, the trap `containers.md` describes. The yield is `durable-objects.md` § *Persist before `ctx.abort()`*: on deployed Cloudflare an abort straight after a write loses it, so a re-claimed slug would inherit the old tenant's chat and source. The next call constructs a fresh object whose `onStart` rebuilds its tables; without the abort, `Resources`, `OrgTree` and the Galaxy's Workspace would stay pointed at dropped tables and a re-created slug would fail with `no such table`.
     - **The hook tells the reset from a failure by what the caller receives, measured first.** The abort rejects the hook's RPC, and Cloudflare documents only that the runtime logs an Error carrying the reason, not what an in-flight caller gets. This phase records that rejection under `wrangler dev` before the hook keys on it, and Phase 16 confirms it deployed.
     - **The entry is observable.** Before it wipes, `teardown()` logs `nebula.scope.teardown` carrying the tier, the cause, `deletion` or `creation`, the binding the entry stamped, and `this.lmz.instanceName` read with no `ctx.id.name` fallback, so a stamp that wrote nothing shows as a missing name.
   - **Every caller moves in the same commit.** `NebulaClient.scopes.expand`, `createGalaxy`, `deletePlan` and `delete` become facade calls through `callAsync`, so the Manage panel and the universe page's create keep working through them, and the universe page's list reads `expandScope` from its own `aud`. `provisionAndLogin`'s galaxy level, `test-helpers.ts`'s `foundStarAndLogin` and `chromium/auth-bootstrap.ts` call the facade's `createGalaxy` through a connected client, `revoke-is-total` calls its `executeScopeDeletion`, and `passage-not-dominion`, the one caller of `create-star`, moves to `claim-star`. The `impersonate` callers in `impersonation-lifecycle`, `impersonation-expiry` and `profile-takeover-refused` take the new signature, each refreshing its parent at the subject's own scope, since the child's `aud` now comes from the caller's.
     - **nebula-auth's own tests move with them.** Every nebula-auth test that calls a deleted route moves to `apps/nebula`'s baseline lane, where the facade is bound, or re-grounds, the must-not-delete acceptance test among them; the route grep below finds them, and one that uses a route only to set up a galaxy creates it through the facade there. `git grep -nE "create-galaxy|create-star|delete-scope|expand-scope|mint-narrower-token" -- 'apps/**/*.ts' 'apps/**/*.vue' 'packages/**/*.ts' ':!**/platform-embed.ts'` returned 145 lines in 38 files on 2026-09-30, and afterwards returns only comments naming a retired route as history, each read; since comments match, the suites passing is the tripwire.
   - **Impersonation keeps its two behaviours across the move** (Larry, 2026-10-01). The mint becomes `parent.lmz.callAsync('NEBULA_AUTH_FACADE', undefined, ctn<NebulaAuthFacade>().impersonate(sub, { ttlSeconds }))`. The facade throws a typed refusal, detected by `err.name` plus a `terminal: true` property (`mesh.md` § *Errors across mesh calls*), and every other rejection stays transient: a paused parent's `callAsync` can reject only with a `TimeoutError` after 30 s, the plain `Error` *LumenizeClient disconnected before the callAsync result arrived*, or a `QuotaExceededError`. A paused parent's mint waits for it to reconnect, so `disconnect()` stays a pause. `ttlSeconds` stays on the mint, which `impersonation-expiry`'s 45-second lapse needs, and leaves the refresh with its body; a test wanting a short plain token uses `vi.setSystemTime`.
   - **Success criteria (capable of failing):**
     - *A refused create never reaches the singleton.* In `/live`, a token without dominion over `acme` calls `createGalaxy('acme.crm')` and is refused with the facade's own message, worded apart from the Registry's *Caller does not have admin access to the parent universe*. Mutation: drop the facade's pre-check, and the refusal that arrives is the Registry's. Positive control: a universe admin's create succeeds.
     - *The universe page's list answers for its own scope.* A token whose `aud` is `acme` calls `expandScope('other-universe')`, and the answer lists `acme`'s apps and nothing of the other universe its holder administers. Mutation: honour the argument, and the other universe's apps appear.
     - *Impersonation refuses an unaccepted subject and mints once they accept.* An invited, never-accepted member is refused, and the same member is minted for after accepting, discriminating on the fixture since the 403 is deliberately collapsed. The acceptance test in `packages/nebula-auth/test/mint-narrower-token.test.ts`, which `security.md` marks must-not-delete, moves with the mint and keeps pinning ADR-012's conjunct. Mutation: drop the acceptance conjunct from the subject read, and the unaccepted subject is minted for.
     - *A deleted scope's Durable Objects are wiped by the server.*
       - In `/live`, an admin deletes an app through the facade, and before anything re-creates it, the same admin's still-valid token reads the deleted Galaxy's chat and finds it empty; a deleted tenant's Star reads empty the same way. The deletion is witnessed first because a re-create's own teardown would empty it either way. Locally miniflare's abort wipes storage by itself, so this limb witnesses the in-memory reset only, and the durable wipe is Phase 16's. Mutations, one per limb: skip the deletion's hook, and the old chat comes back; drop the `ctx.abort()`, and the read fails with `no such table`. Positive control: before the delete, the chat holds the message.
       - The same deletions log one `nebula.scope.teardown` marker with cause `deletion` per Durable Object the deletion names, each carrying its instance name and binding, and no teardown error, counted by the deletion call's id. A universe deleted through the facade logs its own marker beside its galaxies'. A fresh creation's `.dev` Star, whose first touch is the creation's teardown, logs a marker with cause `creation` naming it. Mutations, one per limb: the entry stamps nothing, and the `.dev` Star's marker carries no instance name; count the abort's rejection a failure, and a teardown error is logged; the hook skips the universe tier, and its marker is missing.
       - A new owner starts empty. In `/live`, an admin deletes an app while a second client stays subscribed to its chat; the second client reconnects and re-subscribes, then the admin re-creates the slug through the facade, and the new Galaxy's chat and its subscriber list, read through `subscribeQuerySubscribers` on the Galaxy's `resources` door, are empty. In the stack's stdio, `createGalaxy`'s completion line follows every `creation` marker it caused. Mutations, one per limb: skip the teardown at creation, and the second client's re-created rows survive into the new app; answer before the teardown returns, and the completion line precedes a marker.
       - A refused create tears nothing down. In `/live`, an admin posts in an app's chat, then calls `createGalaxy` on the same slug and is refused with `slug_taken`, matched by message; no `creation` marker carries that call's id, and the message survives. Mutation: tear down whatever the Registry answered.
       - The entry reaches only what `@rawRpc()` decorated. In-lane, since only our own code holds the binding: `rawRpcStub` naming `resourcesResults`, which carries no decorator, is refused by the entry's message. The entry looks up one name, so a dotted path such as `resourcesResults.onOntologyPulled` fails the same check. Mutation: drop the decorator check, and `resourcesResults` is returned. Positive control: `teardown` runs. If the pair measurement says the entry verifies, a `__rawRpc` naming another instance, and one naming the wrong binding, are each refused with the entry's message; mutation: trust the pair.
       - `teardown` is no longer mesh-callable. The three frozen `@mesh()` surface lists, in `scope-isolation.test.ts`, `galaxy-resource-surface.test.ts` and `dev-star-data-lifecycle.test.ts`, drop it, and `scope-isolation.test.ts`'s "was: full admin" premise retires with the test that carries it. Mutation: restore the decorator, and the lists red.
       - The consent path's Profile calls land through the stub. `display-names-reach-subscribers` stays green. Mutation: drop `@rawRpc()` from `setDisplayNames`, and its first limb's nickname never lands.
       - In-lane, since no running system makes one of several calls fail: with one target's `teardown()` throwing, the others still wipe and the error names it. Mutation: let the first failure end the fan-out, and the later targets survive.
     - *A refusal ends a child, and a transport failure does not.* In `/live`, a re-mint after the subject's membership is removed ends the child with `impersonation_ended`, and a re-mint attempted while the parent is paused succeeds once the parent reconnects. The paused limb calls `disconnect()` after the mint is sent, and stays paused past 30 s. Mutations, one per limb: classify the typed refusal as transient, and the child keeps retrying; classify any rejection but the typed refusal as terminal, and the paused parent's child dies.
     - *A paused parent is not a dead one.* `impersonation-lifecycle`'s limb 4 re-grounds on the bug it guards: after `disconnect()` the parent is not torn down, and its first mint after `connect()` succeeds. Mutation: latch teardown on `disconnect()` again, and that mint fails as terminal.
     - *No client names the impersonation scope.* Impersonating a tenant member from that tenant's page mints, and calling `impersonate(sub, { ttlSeconds }, 'acme.crm.t1')` from the galaxy's page is refused, the extra argument ignored. Mutation: honour the argument as the child's `aud`, and the galaxy-page mint succeeds.
     - *A record names the page.* In `/live`, from real refreshes of one universe admin's session, each method called from a page that still holds the authority after Phase 12: `executeScopeDeletion` first from `acme.crm`'s page, deleting that galaxy, then from `acme`'s, deleting another, `createGalaxy` from `acme`, and `impersonate` from the subject's own page. Each record carries `aud` beside `access`, and `impersonate`'s names the subject's `sub` beside the caller's projected claims; each is read from the stack's stdio filtered by the method's call id, with the presence guard. Mutations: drop `aud` from the projection, and the two deletion records' `actingToken` projections, compared alone since `target` and `affected` always differ, are identical; strip the claims from `impersonate`'s record, and it names only the subject; record `createGalaxy` as today, and its line carries neither `access` nor `aud`. Positive control: `WireActingToken` still carries neither `access` nor `aud`.
     - *The Registry no longer answers the deleted routes.* A `POST` straight to the Registry stub's `fetch` at each deleted path, carrying a forged `verifiedAccess: { authScope: '_platform', scopeAdmin: true }`, answers 404. In-lane, since nothing reaches the Registry's `fetch` but the Worker's forward. Mutation: keep one case, and it answers with its old status.
     - *The facade refuses a call with no claims.* In-lane, since no page can produce one: a node's fresh-chain call to any facade method is refused with *requires a verified identity*. Mutation: drop the facade's `onBeforeCall` gate, and the call reaches the method.
     - *No protection is reported missing that nothing uses.* A local boot's Registry start logs no `UNCONFIGURED_PROTECTIONS` error, read from the stack's stdio with the guard `live.md` requires, after waiting for a line the waking request logs later. Mutation: delete the binding and keep its row, and the error line appears.
     - *`create-star` is gone.* `grep -rn 'create-star\|createDevWorkspace\|createStar' apps packages --include='*.ts' --include='*.vue'` returned 51 lines on 2026-09-30 and returns only comments naming a retired route as history, each read, the same end state as the route grep above.
     - *Every lane passes.* `npm test` in `apps/nebula` and `packages/nebula-auth` is green, its `browser` lane included, the `chromium` and `ui-smoke` lanes are green, and `drive.ts all` sweeps green, since the shared helpers move here (`live.md`).
   - **Guidance owned:**
     - **Rules for the bridge.** `mesh.md` § *Node identity is stamped on every first-contact entry*, which gains the `@rawRpc()` entry as a third source, and § *Nebula platform code never drops to raw primitives*, which gains `@rawRpc()`, `rawRpcStub` and the hook seam that rides them and cites ADR-023 for both bridges, stated as a shape, since Phase 15 adds a hook, with its facade paragraph conformed. `mesh.md`'s clause that every `stub.method()` MUST be flagged, and `security.md`'s trust-boundary bullet requiring the receiver to re-validate, each name ADR-023's bridge as the sanctioned exception. `mesh.md`'s `paths:` widen to the nebula-auth files that call `rawRpcStub`, with `CLAUDE.md`'s index row. `workers-projects.md`'s nebula-auth row, which says two subpath exports compose mesh. The `raw-comm.md` header and the `apps/nebula` layer-map row are Phase 10's, which adds the forwards they count.
     - **Rules.** `security.md` rule (1)'s mint-side entry for the root-identity gate, and its delegation rule (2) restated against the facade's `impersonate` with its acceptance pin repointed; its `aud` clause and theorem are Phase 12's. `calibration.md` §5's parameter example and its mint paragraph, which name an `activeScope` the mint no longer takes and a renaming question the facade method answers. `testing.md`'s impersonation example, which names the old signature.
     - **Docs.** `auth.md`'s § *Grants in both planes* note, the facade half of its § *The layers a call passes* note, and § *Impersonation*'s note on `expand-scope`. ADR-007's "Today's code differs" clause on the `@rawRpc()` entry, and ADR-023's note but for its certificate part, which is Phase 15's. In source, `display-names-reach-subscribers`' header and `Profile`'s `#profileId()` JSDoc, which both rest on identity being stamped only on the first mesh entry. `website/docs/nebula/auth-flows.md`'s clauses on `create-galaxy`, `create-star` and the impersonation mint, with `platform-embed.ts` regenerated.

8. **The claim writes an account and its first app, and one owner cannot drain the zone.** `claimUniverse` and `claimUniverseWithTicket` take the first app's slug and write the universe, that galaxy, its `.dev` Star and the unaccepted admin membership at the universe, in one act (§ *Signing up lands in Studio*). `MAX_GALAXIES_PER_OWNER` is counted live in the Registry, over the address's accepted admin memberships less any at `_platform`, at `createGalaxy` and when a claim's membership is accepted (§ *Decisions*). The claim takes slugs only; the names arrive with the universe build (§ *Decisions*).
   - **The refresh record is written where the person is, then reaped if orphaned.** The consume's Workers KV record, acceptance's, and the refresh fallback's healed copy are written by the Worker the person is talking to: `getRefreshRecord` returns the record without writing it. After each put, the Worker asks the Registry for the hash's current record and deletes its put if the row is gone or the values differ, so neither a concurrent revoke nor a concurrent `setIdentityAdmin` loses to a stale put; a reap logs `orphan-reaped` with the `sub` from the record it put, and no acting token, since the revoke already recorded its principal and the reap only rolls back its own request's write. In the Registry, `setIdentityAdmin`'s re-puts check their index row after each put, one local SQL read. `#revokeByHashes`' residual note and `recordSessions`' premise that every session passes through it are re-derived (§ *Constraints*).
   - **A superseded claim retires its whole subtree, and nobody else can be in it.** `#convergePendingClaims` deletes each retired universe's rows by prefix, as `executeScopeDeletion` does, since a claim now writes a galaxy and its `.dev` Star beneath the universe. `claimStar` refuses a parent galaxy whose universe holds no accepted admin membership, with the `parent_not_found` it already answers for an absent parent, since to anyone but its claimant a pending claim's galaxy is not an app yet (§ *Decisions*). `issueInvites` and `createGalaxy` refuse the same way, so a superuser, whose dominion reaches a pending claim, can neither invite into it nor create beneath it, and `issueInvites` also refuses a Star whose founding membership is still pending, since tenants claim their own Stars and nobody has a reason to invite into one before its founder accepts. So no stranger holds a membership, or a session, beneath a claim that convergence retires, and "nothing can be created beneath a universe nobody accepted" holds for every caller. The convergence record names every scope it deletes, as `executeScopeDeletion`'s does, rather than the retired universes alone.
   - **Acceptance is one Registry method, and it says what happened.** `acceptMembership` returns one of four outcomes, accepted, already accepted, refused by the cap, or no such membership, as a reason value rather than a throw (`raw-comm.md` § *Errors over raw Workers RPC*); the last runs no hook, and arises when a deletion lands between the page's load and its Accept. It checks the cap and records; the Worker that puts the refresh record reaps it.
     - **A claim's founding membership is named, never inferred from shape.** It was self-created (no inviter), carries `scopeAdmin`, and sits at a universe or, for a claim-star, at a Star, and never at `_platform`, whose bootstrap membership has the same shape and whose prefix would match every scope.
     - **What a founding membership's acceptance returns.**
       - **On its first acceptance only, the scopes to tear down**, read from the claim's fixed shape: for a universe, the universe and what `Scopes` holds beneath it, which then is exactly the claim's galaxy and `.dev` Star, since nothing can be created beneath a universe nobody accepted; for a claim-star, the Star. The Worker tears them down through the hook before it answers. That Worker serves the founder's own request, the link page's `POST` or Home's Accept, so this defensive wipe is the first call to reach the scope and places it near them (`auth.md` § *Founding a Star*); it must never move into the Registry or anything placed beside it.
       - **For a universe's membership, accepted or already, every live galaxy beneath the universe**, to order a certificate for, since ordering is idempotent and nothing stores which galaxy the claim wrote; a deleted galaxy's row is gone, so it is never named.
       - **The router's hooks become required here** (§ *Which host answers what*). The recording stub goes to every call `git grep -n 'routeNebulaAuthRequest(' -- packages/nebula-auth/test` finds, 19 on 2026-10-02, the real hooks to `apps/nebula/src/entrypoint.ts`'s, and the package README's example gains them.
     - **What it records.** Each acceptance logs its record: the credential that proved the mailbox, the link or a refresh cookie, the address, the `sub`s accepted, and the scopes it hands to teardown, with no acting token (ADR-016). The Registry logs it before the Worker tears down, so it names what was ordered, and the hook's failure line names any target it missed. Today it answers `{ accepted: [] }` both for a membership that does not exist and for one already accepted, so the Worker cannot tell an order still owed from a refusal. From Phase 10 both writers call it: the link page's `POST` and Home's `accept-membership`.
   - **Every claim caller sends the first app's slug** (Larry, 2026-10-02). Both in-app forms gain the field: the login page's create-account mode (`LoginScreen.vue`) and the ticket-backed signup page (`SignupScreen.vue`), each asking for "Account" and then "Your first app" and posting both slugs. The shared-pages item later adds display names and decides whether an app brings its own page, without changing what the forms send. The test callers move in the same commit: `requestUniverseClaim` behind `provisionAndLogin`, nebula-auth's `test-helpers.ts`, the `browser` and `chromium` setups, and the scenarios. `git grep -nE "claim-universe|/auth/signup|claimUniverse" -- 'apps/**/*.ts' 'apps/**/*.vue' 'packages/**/*.ts' ':!**/platform-embed.ts'` returned 134 lines in 38 files on 2026-10-02, each read. `signup-to-first-app` re-grounds: its first limb fills both fields, and its limbs on an empty account, which the claim's first app makes false, re-ground on the universe page listing that app and opening its Studio on a live session.
   - **Both claims validate and record what they write.** Both claim paths validate the first app's slug as they do the account's. Each claim records the address, the membership it opened, the universe, the galaxy and its `.dev` Star, the ticket-backed claim included; `claimStar`, which this phase edits, records the address, the minted membership's `sub` and the Star, where today it logs only the scope and the address. The consume records the address it proved and every membership it opened, with no acting token (ADR-016).
   - **Success criteria (capable of failing):**
     - *The claim writes three scopes.* A claim for `acme` and `crm` leaves `Scopes` rows for `acme`, `acme.crm` and `acme.crm.dev`, and one unaccepted admin membership at `acme`. Mutation: write only the universe, and two rows are missing.
     - *One owner cannot pass the cap, at either site.* In a local `/live` scenario, an owner creates galaxies through the facade up to the cap, the next `createGalaxy` is refused with the cap's message and writes no `Scopes` row, and accepting a universe claim from the same address whose first galaxy would cross it is refused the same way, leaving the membership unaccepted. No `creation` marker carries either refused call's id. Mutations, one per site: drop the check at `createGalaxy`, and the create succeeds; drop it at acceptance, and the claim is accepted; tear down whatever the Registry answered, and the refused create logs markers. Positive control: the create just below the cap succeeds.
     - *A claim's first acceptance starts its scopes empty, and no later one touches them.* Each limb counts `creation` markers by its own request's id.
       - In `/live`, a claim for `acme` and `crm` is accepted, and markers carrying its id name each of `acme`, `acme.crm` and `acme.crm.dev`; the acceptance's completion line follows all three. A claim-star for `acme.crm.t1`, accepted, logs one marker naming the Star. Mutations, one per limb: skip the teardown at first acceptance, and the markers are missing; tear down universe claims only, and the Star's marker is missing; answer before the teardown returns, and the completion line precedes a marker.
       - The person posts a message in Studio, and accepting the same membership again from Home logs no marker carrying its id and leaves the message in place. Mutation: tear down on already accepted too, and the message is gone.
       - The bootstrap address's first Accept at `_platform` logs no marker carrying its id, and another account's chat survives it; an invitee's first Accept logs none either, and the invited app's chat survives it. Mutations, one per limb: treat any self-created admin membership as founding, and `_platform`'s Accept tears down by its prefix; tear down on any first acceptance, and the invitee's Accept logs markers.
     - *Acceptance tells its outcomes apart.* In-lane, since the outcome is a value passed between the Registry and the Worker until Phase 15's marker observes it: accepting a claim's membership returns accepted with its galaxy, a second Accept returns already accepted with the galaxy, an Accept past the cap returns refused with the cap's message, and an Accept for a membership a deletion removed returns no such membership. On a fixture whose universe holds a second galaxy, the same Accept after the claim's galaxy is deleted names only the second. Mutations, one per limb: answer already accepted as accepted, and the second limb reds; return galaxies only on a first acceptance, and the second galaxy is not named; throw on the cap, and the refusal arrives as an error rather than an outcome; answer a missing membership as already accepted, and the last limb reds.
     - *Every acceptance leaves its record.* In `/live`, filtered by the acceptance's id with the presence guard, and matched by its namespace, `nebula-auth.Registry.identity.membershipAccepted`, since the claim's and the mailbox's own lines name the address too: a claim's acceptance records the proving credential, the address, the `sub` accepted and the scopes it hands to teardown, with no acting token. Mutations: drop the record, and no line carries the namespace; omit the scopes handed to teardown, and the record names none of the claim's three. Phase 10 re-runs it through both writers.
     - *A stranger's claims cannot fill your count.* `MAX_GALAXIES_PER_OWNER` claims name X's address and are never consumed; X's own claim, consume and acceptance then succeed. Mutation: count unaccepted memberships, and X's acceptance is refused with the cap's message.
     - *A superuser's root membership is not counted.* The count's prefix scan takes the platform arm, `%`, for a membership at `_platform`, as `#childLevel` does, which is why the root is left out; a `LIKE` built from a scope escapes `_`. A superuser holding an accepted membership at `_platform` creates a galaxy in a universe they admin while more galaxies than the cap exist elsewhere. Mutation: count the root through that arm, and the create is refused with the cap's message.
     - *Nothing enters a scope its founder has not accepted.* In `/live`, under a claim for `acme` and `crm` that nobody has accepted: a `claim-star` for `acme.crm.t1` is refused with the `parent_not_found` message, a superuser's invite into `acme.crm` is refused with its own message, and a superuser's `createGalaxy('acme.web')` is refused with `parent_not_found`. Under an accepted app, a galaxy admin's invite into a tenant Star whose founder has not accepted is refused with the same family's message. Mutations, one per limb: drop the accepted-universe check from `claimStar`, from `issueInvites`, from `createGalaxy`, or the pending-Star check from `issueInvites`, and that call succeeds. Positive controls: once the universe claim is accepted, the same `claim-star`, invite and create succeed, and once the tenant's founder accepts, the same invite lands.
     - *A superseded claim leaves nothing behind.* Two claims from one address, one of them accepted, leave no `Scopes` row under the retired slug, and a later claim of that slug with the same first app succeeds. The convergence record, matched by its debug namespace, names `acme2`, `acme2.crm` and `acme2.crm.dev`. Mutations, one per limb: delete by the exact universe name, and `acme2.crm` survives and the re-claim fails; record the universes alone, and the record lacks the galaxy.
     - *A put whose index row is gone, or whose values changed, is reaped.* In-lane, since no running system can time a revoke into that gap: each of the three steps runs through its real call site, with `runInDurableObject` deleting the hash's index row, or flipping its `scopeAdmin`, between the Registry's answer and the Worker's put. Each leaves no KV record and logs `orphan-reaped`. A fourth limb drives `setIdentityAdmin`'s re-put the same way, deleting the hash's index row while its put is in flight. Mutations, one per step: skip the check, and the record survives; compare existence only, and the stale `scopeAdmin` survives. Positive control: with the row unchanged, the record stays and the next refresh mints.
     - *Both forms send the first app's slug.* In `/live`, `signup-to-first-app`'s first limb fills the login page's two fields and the claim leaves three `Scopes` rows. A new limb there takes the ticket path in the same browser: it consumes a link for an address with no memberships, fills the rendered signup page's two fields, submits, and finds three `Scopes` rows, since `signup-one-email` posts its ticket claim from Node and nothing else renders `SignupScreen.vue`. A Node `POST` to `/auth/claim-universe` without the first app's slug is refused with the slug message. Mutations, one per limb: drop the field from the login page, and the claim is refused; drop it from the signup page, and the ticket claim is refused; accept a claim without it, and the `POST` writes a universe alone. Positive control: the same `POST` with both slugs writes three rows.
