     - *The first app's slug is validated at both claim paths.* A 31-character slug, one containing `--` and one with an uppercase letter are each refused with the slug message, and no `Scopes` row is written. Mutation: skip the check, and `acme.Bad_Slug` is written. Positive control: `crm` passes.
     - *A claim and a consume each leave their record.* In `/live`, filtered by each request's id with the presence guard, and each record matched by its namespace, the claim's `nebula-auth.Registry.claimUniverse`, `claimUniverseWithTicket` or `claimStar` and the consume's `nebula-auth.Registry.login.established`: the claim's record names the address, the membership it opened, and all three scopes; the consume's names the proved address and every membership it opened, and carries no acting token. A ticket-backed claim, made by consuming a link for an address with no memberships and then posting `/auth/signup`, leaves the same record. Mutations: log only the universe, and the galaxy and `.dev` Star are missing; drop the consume's record, and no line carries its namespace; drop the ticket claim's record, and its line is missing; log `claim-star` as today, and its line names no `sub`.
   - **Guidance owned:** `docs/vision/auth.md` § *Founding a Star*'s "Today's code differs" note, whose two clauses, the accepted-universe refusal in step 1 and the Accept's teardown as the first touch in step 3, this phase makes true. `.claude/rules/security.md`'s promise that no token is ever live but unindexed, now kept by the reap; the fallback's rationale is Phase 10's, which changes the fallback again. [nebula-pre-alpha.md](nebula-pre-alpha.md) § *Scope full names*, whose "galaxy creation sits elsewhere and needs finding" stops being true for a first galaxy, and whose `name` column on `Scopes` the universe build replaces with scope-metadata resources; and § *Shared pages*, which names no first-app field. A grep of that file for the old sentence finds nothing.

9. **The deployment names its origin once** (§ *The deployment names its origin once*). One `wrangler.jsonc` var holds the origin, `local-config.mjs` rewrites it for the local stack beside the `routes` it strips, and one parse reads ADR-021's whole grammar, persona labels included, with the refusals § *The deployment names its origin once* states; it validates each host label with `isValidSlug` and never with `parseId`, which admits the root. `NEBULA_AUTH_ISSUER` and `NebulaAuthFacade`'s fallback origin derive from it: `iss` becomes a parameter of the payload builder and the test mint, threaded from `env` at the Worker's call sites, since the pure `/claims` subpath has no `env`. The parse lives in a pure module exported through a Node-safe subpath, for vite's config and the harness to import, and `mintDegradedToken` signs with the derived `iss`. `workers_dev` becomes `false`, and production's routes become `*.lumenize.dev/*` and the apex. `deploy-test.sh` deploys with `lumenize-test.dev`'s routes and origin, its own KV namespace, and every `services[].service` rewritten to its worker name; `deploy.sh`'s preflight hint stops copying keys from `.dev.vars`.
   - **Success criteria (capable of failing):**
     - *The parse reads the grammar and refuses the rest.* A table test — pool-workers is right here, since this is a parser — maps `tenant1.crm.acme.lumenize.localhost:54321` to `acme.crm.tenant1`, `manny--dev.crm.acme.lumenize.dev` to persona `manny` in `acme.crm.dev`, and `platform.lumenize.dev` to the platform host; and refuses `x.tenant1.crm.acme.lumenize.dev`, a 31-character label, `a--b--c.crm.acme.lumenize.dev`, `manny--tenant1.crm.acme.lumenize.dev`, a persona under a Star that is no environment, `_platform.lumenize.dev`, `x._platform.lumenize.dev` and `evil.example`. Mutations: drop the depth check, and the four-deep host parses; validate the joined scope with `parseId`, and `_platform.lumenize.dev` parses to the root.
     - *A deployment accepts only its own tokens.* A token minted through the real payload builder under origin A fails verification under origin B. Mutation: restore the constant, and it verifies. Positive control: the same token verifies under A.
     - *The parse imports without the runtime.* `vite.config.ts` and the harness import the parse, and the `/claims` subpath's graph still holds no `cloudflare:workers`. Mutation: import the parse from the package's main entry, and the Node import fails.
     - *The test target never reaches production.* `deploy-test.sh`'s generated config binds `REFRESH_TOKEN_KV` to an explicit id read from `.dev.vars`, never an id-less binding, which wrangler would fill with the deployed worker's, and never `wrangler.jsonc`'s; and it names no service `"nebula"`. The script refuses to run without that id. Mutations: drop either rewrite, and the matching id or name reappears; emit the binding without an id, and the check reds.

10. **Every session lives on the platform host, and every scope is served from its own host.** The switch, landed in one phase because a cookie that moves without its pages, or pages without their cookie, leaves nobody able to log in. In four parts:
    - **Serving** (§ *Which host answers what*). The Worker tries the platform host's routes, then the routes every host answers by path, then a page chosen by host; the platform host's routes live in a `WorkerEntrypoint` reached by a self-referencing binding. `run_worker_first` becomes `true`, and the apex redirects to the platform host. `/app/` goes: the Galaxy serves a built app by its Star's host, and `nebula-scope` drops both scope fields and gains whether the page is the dev Star and the origin a page may post its auth state to. vite's `authAppRoutes` and `appSpaFallback` decide by the one parse. `/auth/*` on any host but the platform host answers 404 before the page step. The local stack answers on `*.lumenize.localhost`, with `--host-resolver-rules` for Chromium and a `lookup` hook for Node's `fetch`, so Linux answers like macOS.
      - **Locally, vite serves the link pages.** Under the harness the Worker's assets layer is empty by design (`browser.ts` and ui-smoke's global setup create an empty dist), so `serveAuthApp` answers 503 there. vite's auth-screen list, which already serves login, signup, the emails page and Home, gains the magic-link page, and a scenario mints its links through vite's port, so the link it follows is served by vite as sent. That is the dev server serving what Workers Assets serves in production, as § *Decisions* records for the rest of the list.
      - **Page traffic rides one track into a Durable Object** (§ *What reaches a Durable Object's `fetch`*). The page step forwards through one helper that rewrites the path into `/_public/{path}`, strips every client-sent `x-lumenize-*` header before setting its own, refuses an `Upgrade`, and answers anything but `GET` and `HEAD` with a 405 carrying `Allow: GET, HEAD`, as `serveAppForward` does today. A Durable Object's `onRequest` dispatches `/_public/` and the paths its own container's library fixes, and answers 404 to the rest. `npm run audit:do-http` lands here, with `raw-comm.md`'s rule.
      - **Every HTML response carries `frame-ancestors`** — `'none'` on the platform host, a universe page and Studio, the galaxy's Studio origin on a Star's or persona's host — `serveAuthApp`'s responses included, and vite's two middlewares stamp the same through the shared parse.
      - **vite forwards every method on a Star or persona host to the Worker**, so a limb that probes a Durable Object's surface reaches the Worker, and such a limb hits the Worker's port directly.
      - **The root stops parsing.** `parseId`'s admission of `_platform` goes with the body parses Phase 1 kept it for, and `requirePassage` checks `isPlatformScope` before it parses, which Phase 12's refusal of its own relies on.
      - **The `/gateway/` upgrade rests on its token.** `routeDORequest`'s server-side `Origin` check on an upgrade reads `LUMENIZE_APPROVED_ORIGINS`, which production sets to `""`; with the var gone the upgrade is guarded by the token, which rides the subprotocol and which no cross-site page can obtain.
    - **Sessions** (§ *Sessions*, ADR-022).
      - **Cookies and the `POST` rule.** The consume sets `__Host-refresh-token.{scope}` at `Path=/`, `SameSite=Lax`, on the platform host. Every `POST` to `/auth/` but the refresh requires `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin`, and `LUMENIZE_APPROVED_ORIGINS` goes with the test rig's rewrite and `auth-flows.md` § *Cross-origin browser deploys*.
      - **The refresh.** It reads no body and no `Content-Type`, takes the host's scope from `Origin`, reads the candidate cookies broadest first, keeps only those whose record's own scope sits at or above the host's, mints only on an accepted membership on either arm, falls back once to the Registry on a KV miss, and expires a cookie that misses both. It is the only route that answers CORS, for that origin alone; the router-wide wrapper goes.
      - **Login, links and consent.** Every emailed link that carries a token is a magic link: `InviteTokens` held four of `MagicLinks`' five columns and differed only in lifetime and `purpose`, so `InviteTokens` goes and an invite mints a `MagicLinks` row that lives 7 days, against 30 minutes for a sign-in or a claim (§ *Decisions*). `POST /auth/email-magic-link` checks `return_to` through the parse before it stores it in the record's `returnTo`, the login page only passing it along, and the consume redirects there. The server sets it for a claim, to the first galaxy's Studio host, and for an invite, to the invite's host. Every link is `GET /auth/magic-link?token=…`, which opens a page and changes nothing; `accept-invite` and its `invite_token` go, and `one_time_token` becomes `token`. `executeScopeDeletion` and `#convergePendingClaims` delete from `MagicLinks` alone.
        - **The table changes.** Migrations 23 and 24 drop and rebuild `MagicLinks` as V3, as 17 and 18 did, adding `returnTo`, a nullable `spentAt` that the spend sets and the lookup reports, and a `purpose` that admits `'invite'`, so the activity log still tells an invite from a sign-in; migration 25 drops `InviteTokens`. Links outstanding at deploy are lost, which the wipe makes moot.
        - **A re-invite links to the scope's host.** `invite-entry.ts`'s `invite-existing` mail, sent to someone already accepted, carries no token and links to the invited scope's own host, spelled through § *The deployment names its origin once*’s parse; `landing.ts`'s `/app` and `/{scope}` path prefixes go.
        - **The page reads before anything is consumed.** It shows the address, and for a pending membership the scope, whether it came by invite, `invitedByName`, and the nickname and name to pre-fill. Today those come from `pending-membership`, whose cookie the `GET` consume set. They now come from `POST /auth/magic-link/lookup`, which takes `{ token }`, writes no Registry state, and answers the address, the pending membership at the link's scope if there is one, and whether the link is spent. `resolveConsume` splits into that pure lookup and the consume that proves the mailbox, so `emailVerified` and the bootstrap membership are written only by the consuming `POST`, and a scanner that runs a page's JavaScript proves nothing. The lookup reads display names only for an address that already holds an accepted membership, so a scanner never creates a new invitee's Profile near itself. The lookup and the consuming `POST` both carry `connectionRateLimitGuard`, as the `GET` consume does today.
        - **The page's `POST` consumes.** `POST /auth/magic-link` carries the token, and for a pending membership the nickname and name `handleAcceptMembership` reads from its body today. It is the consent screen's Accept for a link that opens a pending membership, which also accepts through the Registry's one acceptance method (Phase 8), and "Continue as {address}" for a plain login (§ *Passage and dominion read the host's scope*).
          - **The consume refuses a spent token itself**, with the used-link message, and sets no cookie. The page hides Continue for a spent link, but a token from browser history or a forwarded mail can be posted without the page, and today's `resolveConsume` checks only that the row exists and has not expired.
          - **When the link is spent.** It spends the link once it has recorded the sessions, whatever the acceptance then answers, so a click that fails before that can be retried and a refused Accept leaves nothing to replay; Home's Accept retries a refused acceptance. A consume that finds no membership spends the link when it issues the signup ticket. The page drops the token from its URL with `history.replaceState` once it has read it, and the read reports a spent link, whose page says the link was used and offers sign-in. An Accept the cap refuses still proves the mailbox and sets the cookies a consume sets, leaves the membership pending, and shows the cap's message on the page. Both acceptance writers, this `POST` and Home's `accept-membership`, call one Worker helper that puts and reaps the refresh records.
      - **Home and logout.** Home reads by cookie through `POST /auth/home-summary`; `scope-summary`, `NebulaClient.scopes.summary()`, `verifyJwtGuard` and `forwardWithSubject` with its `act` refusal go, and so does the Registry's `scope-summary` fetch case, which answers from a subject the request body supplies. One logout route ends every cookie the browser holds, recording each `sub` whose sessions it ended, and takes `everywhere`, which ends every record of each address those cookies name; `logout-all` goes, and `logout-all-and-writers.test.ts`'s four acceptance-writer arms move onto the new route. Both classify a cookie's name as the refresh does, with `isPlatformScope` beside `parseId`, and resolve at most the bounded count § *Constraints* gives; the logout expires every refresh cookie it received regardless.
    - **The client and Studio** (§ *Client*). `NebulaClient` refreshes at the platform host with `credentials: 'include'` and no body; a 401 sends a top-level page to log in with `return_to`, and a framed page posts once to its parent at the injected origin instead. `CreateNebulaClientConfig` drops `authScope` and `activeScope`, and the `nebula.authScope:` hint goes with its readers; the client takes its active scope from its first token's `aud`, and a call made before the first refresh waits on `ready`. `logout()` sends the tab to the platform host's logout page, and in a frame tears the client down and tells the parent. Studio reads its scope from the host through `view-state.ts`, moves between scopes by navigating to hosts, frames the dev Star's host, and ignores a message from any origin but the frames it created. The Manage panel, `?manage`, "Log out of this app", the menu's "Log out everywhere", `stageMode 'landing'` and the `localStorage` return go, and ui-smoke's *create an app from the manage panel* retargets at the universe page's create form, which exists today.
    - **The venues** (§ *Venues*). `email-login.ts`, `harness.ts`, `browser.ts` and `prod-drive.ts` log in on the platform host through the Browser shim and refresh against it; `connectDriver` passes no scopes. ui-smoke's global setup and helpers move to the hosts. Every scenario is re-grounded here, so the sweep is green from this commit on: each limb whose observation this build deletes is re-grounded on what replaces it, or deleted with its reason recorded in the scenario's header, never deleted to green the sweep, and `superuser-end-to-end`'s limb 2 re-grounds on a superuser's token at a universe's page, whose `authScope` is the platform root and whose `aud` is that universe. The vitest lanes move with them: both `test-helpers.ts` files, `identity-mint-point.test.ts`'s fallback and revoke tests and `mint-all-and-acceptance.test.ts` onto the platform host's refresh, and the `browser` and `chromium` lanes onto these hosts. The `chromium` lane serves its page from a scope host, `crm.acme.lumenize.localhost:<port>`, so the refresh's `Origin` parses, and follows a magic link by a top-level navigation, then clicks the page's Continue; its proxy's rewrite of `Origin` and `Path=` goes with the need for it.
    - **Success criteria (capable of failing):**
      - *A page gets an access token for its own host and no other.* A `/live` browser scenario opens pages on two hosts; each refresh answers that host's `aud` with CORS naming only its origin, and a page on the platform host gets nothing. The fixture is the superuser holding the `_platform` membership. Mutation: drop the lookup's check that the host names a scope, and the platform host's page gets a token.
      - *The refresh is a simple request.* It succeeds cross-origin with only safelisted request headers. Mutation: restore the `Content-Type` check, and it is refused before the handler.
      - *The refresh reads only cookies at or above the host's scope.* In `/live`, read from the stack's stdio filtered by each refresh's id, with the presence guard. Nomination: one address holding a plain membership at `acme.crm.tenant1` and an accepted admin membership in another universe; on the tenant's host the per-candidate read marker names only the tenant's cookie. Mutation: treat every `__Host-refresh-token.*` cookie as a candidate, and the other universe's read appears. Early exit: an admin at `acme` who is also a plain member at `acme.crm.tenant1`; the marker names only `acme`. Mutation: read every candidate, and the tenant's read appears.
      - *A cookie's name nominates it, and its record decides.* In `/live`, after a real login as the admin of a universe `evil`, a Node `fetch` sends that admin's own refresh value as `__Host-refresh-token.acme`, with `Origin` naming `tenant1.crm.acme.lumenize.localhost`, and gets 401, matched as the refresh's own refusal rather than a 500. Mutation: take the candidate's scope from its name, and a token with `authScope: acme` comes back. Positive control: the same value under its true name, `__Host-refresh-token.evil`, mints on an `evil` host.
      - *The refresh picks the admin membership, not the plain one.* A scopeAdmin at `acme` who is a plain member of `acme.crm.tenant1` gets `authScope: acme` on the tenant's host, and holds dominion over the tenant itself. Mutation: pick the plain membership, and a dominion-gated call on the tenant is refused, matched by message.
      - *Someone holding admin memberships at a universe and at one of its galaxies carries the universe membership's `sub` on both pages.* Mutation: pick the nearest, and the id changes between the two.
      - *Only an accepted membership mints, on either arm.* Each pending membership here is an invitee's who signed in through a plain magic link without accepting, since closing the consent screen sets no cookie. A pending invited admin at `acme` beside an accepted plain membership at the page mints on the plain one; an invited, never-accepted member alone at the page gets 401, matched as `membership_not_accepted`. Mutations, one per arm: drop the accepted filter from the pick, and the pending admin's token comes back; drop it from the fallback, and the never-accepted member mints. Positive control: the same member, after acceptance, gets a token.
      - *A KV miss with a live index row still mints.* In-lane, porting `identity-mint-point.test.ts`'s fallback test, since the miss lives in KV's propagation window, a timing window no scenario can hit: with only the KV record deleted, the refresh answers 200, sets no `Max-Age=0` for that cookie and heals the record, and the next refresh answers 200. Mutation: expire the cookie on the first KV miss, and the second refresh 401s.
      - *A cookie without the `__Host-` prefix is not a candidate.* Log in as A in one browser context and read A's raw `__Host-refresh-token.acme` through Playwright's `context.cookies()`. In a **fresh context holding none of our cookies**, from a scope host, plant `refresh-token.acme=<A's value>; Domain=lumenize.localhost; Path=/` and assert the platform host's refresh answers 401. Mutation: accept the unprefixed name, and the plant mints A's token in a browser that never logged in. Positive control: the plant is in the context's cookie list before the refresh, since this is the `Domain=` plant's first measurement over local `http`, and a plant that never landed would pass for the wrong reason. The planted name carries the scope suffix and the context is fresh for the same reason.
      - *A revoked membership's cookie stops costing a Registry read.* After a removal, the next refresh carrying that cookie answers with it expired, and the response's `Set-Cookie` carries `Secure`; the refresh after that carries it no longer. Mutation: drop the expiry, and the cookie is still presented on the third refresh.
      - *The `Sec-Fetch-Site` check refuses the right request, and says which mechanism refused.* On any `POST` to `/auth/` but the refresh, same-origin passes and cross-site is refused; the refresh also passes same-site; a header-less `POST` passes unless its `Origin` is one the route would refuse; a proxied browser login through `wrangler dev` and vite passes. Match by message or the debug sink. Mutation: drop the check, and the cross-site leg passes.
      - *Loading or following someone's link signs nobody in.* In `/live`, A requests a magic link and an invite, and the harness takes both from the real email.
        - A second browser context, holding none of A's cookies, opens a page on the dev Star's host, where Playwright, standing in for generated code, adds A's magic link and the invite link as `<img>`s. It waits until the network log shows both image requests completed, then sends the top window to A's magic link. The context's cookie jar gains nothing, and the navigation lands on "Continue as A". Mutations, one per limb: consume on the `GET`, and the image signs the context in as A; accept on the invite's `GET`, and the invite's membership is accepted; consume on the navigation's `GET`, and it signs in without a click. Positive controls: each image request appears in the network log as completed, and clicking Continue signs the context in as A.
        - Rendering the link's page proves nothing. A is an address invited and never signed in. Before Continue, the stack's stdio holds no `mailboxProved` line for A, filtered by the lookup's id with the presence guard; `resolveConsume` logs it only when it flips the address to proved. Mutation: resolve the lookup through the proving consume, and the line appears. Positive control: after Continue, A's `mailboxProved` line appears.
        - The invite's page, opened as sent in a context with an empty cookie jar, shows the inviter's name, and pre-fills a nickname only for an address that holds an accepted membership. Three invitees, each opening their own invite:
          - B accepted a membership in another account and set the nickname `Bee`, and B's page pre-fills it.
          - A, invited and never signed in, sees the inviter's name and an empty field.
          - C accepted, set a nickname, and deleted that account before this invite, so C holds a nickname but no accepted membership, and C's page pre-fills nothing. A's Profile is empty either way, so only C can tell a conditional read from an unconditional one.
          - Mutations: read through `pending-membership`, and B's page has nothing to show; read display names unconditionally, and C's page pre-fills C's old nickname. Positive control: B's pre-fill.
      - *A re-invite lands on its scope's host.* In `/live`, an accepted member is invited again, follows the email's link as sent, and lands on the invited scope's host. Mutation: keep `landingBase`, and the link lands on a path this phase deleted.
      - *A login returns to the page that sent it, fragment included, in another browser.* A link carrying a fragment, opened signed out, logs in through a magic link followed in a second browser context and lands on the same URL. Mutation: drop `return_to` from the record, and the second context lands on Home.
      - *A `return_to` off the site is refused where it is stored.* A Node `POST` straight to `/auth/email-magic-link`, carrying a scheme-relative `//evil.example`, a `javascript:` or `data:` URL, or a host the parse refuses, is answered 400 and stores no record; the page navigates only to a `return_to` the server validated. Mutation: validate only in the login page, and the `POST` stores `//evil.example` and the consume redirects off-site. Positive control: a scope host's URL is kept.
      - *A host the parse refuses is refused explicitly.* A page load on a four-deep host answers 404 and its refresh 403, never 500 — `superuser-end-to-end` limb 5's property against the host. Mutation: let the parse throw, and both answer 500.
      - *A minted link follows the request it was minted from.* A magic link and an invite link minted from requests on two different ports each land on the platform host at that port. Mutation: build the link from a constant origin, and one goes red.
      - *The Registry no longer answers `scope-summary`.* In-lane, since nothing reaches the Registry's `fetch` but the Worker's forward: a `POST` straight to the Registry stub's `fetch` at `/auth/scope-summary`, carrying a forged `verifiedProfileId`, answers 404. Mutation: keep the case, and it answers the forged subject's summary.
      - *No route of ours reads a token but `PUT /pictures`.* A test walks the route table and finds no entry under `/auth/` verifying a Bearer token, and nothing returns a person's whole summary to a token. Mutation: restore `scope-summary`, and the walk reds.
      - *"Log out" ends every host in the browser.* After it, pages on two hosts that different memberships serve both land on the platform host's login when reloaded. Mutation: end only the cookie behind the page that asked, and the other comes back signed in. A second limb, with a superuser holding the `_platform` cookie beside a universe membership: Home lists the platform row before the logout, and afterwards a universe host's refresh answers 401. Mutation: classify names with `parseId` alone, and the platform cookie survives the logout and still mints.
      - *A sign-out inside the dev tab leaves Studio's session alive.* The framed client's `logout()` posts its teardown to the parent, and the top window's URL is unchanged. Mutation: navigate the top window, and Studio is replaced.
      - *A derived client's logout ends only itself.* In-lane, porting `impersonate-lifetime.test.ts`'s probe of the scoped route, since the observable after this phase is a navigation the child must not make, and `/live` drives clients from Node, where `logout()` has no window to navigate: an impersonating child's `logout()` sends no request and navigates nowhere, and the admin's platform refresh still answers 200. Mutation: delete the `#mintedFrom` branch, and the spy sees the navigation. The navigation spy is the observable; the cookie probe is kept as a regression guard, since `logout()` no longer posts.
      - *Logging out records whose sessions ended.* In `/live`, logging out a browser holding two memberships logs a revocation naming each `sub` with reason `logout`, filtered by the logout's id with the presence guard. Mutation: batch the hashes with no subject, and the line names neither.
      - *A frame that cannot get a token posts once and leaves the tab where it is*, and Studio ignores a message from any origin but the frames it created. Mutation: navigate the tab from the frame, and Studio is replaced by a login page carrying the frame's own `return_to`. The fixture is built for it: a dev tab whose holder's `.dev` membership was removed, since a plain galaxy member's tab gets a token from the co-minted `.dev` membership. Further limbs, each with its own mutation: a page with no injected origin posts nothing (mutation: post to `'*'`); Studio ignores a message from an origin it did not frame (mutation: accept any origin).
      - *An invite is a magic link that lives a week.* In `/live`, an invite's email links to `/auth/magic-link?token=…` and its page shows the consent card. In-lane, since the lifetime is a value the mint writes and waiting out a week proves nothing more: an invite's `MagicLinks` row expires 7 days after issue, and a sign-in's 30 minutes. Mutation: mint an invite with the sign-in lifetime, and its row expires in 30 minutes.
      - *A link signs in once.* Each replay runs in a fresh context, so only the server's record of the spend can refuse it.
        - In `/live`, an invite's Accept and a magic link's Continue each sign the context in, and the same link replayed finds the page saying it was used, and the context's cookie jar gains nothing. After the page loads, its URL carries no token. Mutations: keep the invite link multi-use, and the replay signs in; keep the magic link multi-use, and the same; drop `history.replaceState`, and the URL keeps the token. Positive control: an unspent link still opens its page and signs in.
        - An Accept the cap refused, replayed, finds the page saying it was used, and the jar gains nothing. Mutation: leave a cap-refused link unspent, and its replay signs in.
        - A link for an address with no memberships, followed and then replayed, finds the page saying it was used and sets no signup-ticket cookie. Mutation: spend only where sessions are recorded, and the replay is issued a fresh ticket.
        - Past the page, a Node `POST` to `/auth/magic-link` carrying `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin` and a spent token, once for a magic link and once for an invite, is refused with the used-link message and sets no cookie. Mutation: let the consume ignore `spentAt` while the lookup still reports it, and the `POST` signs in. Positive control: the same `POST` before the spend signs in.
        - In-lane, since no running system makes `recordSessions` fail: with it throwing, a retry of the same link signs in. Mutation: spend before recording, and the retry finds the link used.
      - *The link page's Accept is an acceptance writer like Home's.* In `/live`, an owner already at the cap claims another account and opens the claim's link as sent. Its Accept shows the cap's message on the page, matched by message, the claim's membership stays pending, and the cookies for the address's accepted memberships are set. Mutation: let the `POST` accept without the Registry's acceptance method, and the claim is accepted past the cap. Positive control: below the cap, the same Accept lands in Studio. Phase 8's reap criterion and its acceptance-record criterion re-run here through both writers, the merged `POST` and Home's `accept-membership`. The record is written in the one Registry method, so dropping it reds both writers at once; what each writer supplies is the proving credential. Mutation, per writer: hand the Registry the other writer's credential, and only that writer's record names the wrong one.
      - *A 401 with a pending membership offers its consent.* A person invited at `acme.crm` who signs in through a plain magic link without accepting, then visits the invite's host and is sent to the platform host by a 401, sees that membership's consent rather than the login. Mutation: show the login, and the person loops. A second limb: an invite's `return_to` is the invite's scope, set by the server, so a link whose query carries another `return_to` still lands there. Mutation: take `return_to` from the link, and it lands elsewhere.
      - *A change Studio makes to the dev Star reaches the dev tab as a push, with no reload.* Mutation: restore Phase 2's fence, and the push is refused.
      - *A stored picture renders across hosts.* A picture uploaded on a tenant host renders on the platform host and on a galaxy host from the same stored value, and each `<img>`'s resolved `src` names its own page's host. Mutation: store the absolute URL, and the `src` names the uploading host.
      - *A page load on every host reaches its page.* Locally, vite hands each host in § *Which host answers what*'s table the page it names, through the shared parse. Mutation: bypass the parse in `appSpaFallback`, and a Star host gets Studio. (The `run_worker_first` half is Phase 16's, since the assets layer is inert under `wrangler dev`.)
      - *Each host is framed only by the origin it names.* Against the Worker's port, the response to a page load on the platform host, a universe page and Studio carries `frame-ancestors 'none'`, and on the dev Star's host the galaxy's Studio origin, asserted on the headers, since the local Worker has no dist to frame. Mutation: drop the Worker's stamp, and the header is missing. Then, in `/live` through vite's port, a signed-in context opens the dev Star's host, so its client holds a token and stays put, and Playwright adds two iframes to that page, standing in for generated code, one of Home and one of Studio. Both are blocked: Chromium shows its error frame rather than the page. Mutation: drop vite's stamp, and the Home frame renders. Positive control: Studio's own frame of the dev tab loads. The Worker's stamp is framed for real on the deployed target (Phase 16).
      - *The page helper keeps a request on its track.* In-lane, since the helper is a pure request-to-request function: a `GET` carrying `x-lumenize-planted`, a header the helper never sets, arrives without it; an `Upgrade` is refused with the helper's message; a `POST` is answered 405 with `Allow: GET, HEAD`; and `/api` arrives as `/_public/api`. Mutations, one per limb: skip the strip, and the planted header arrives; drop the `Upgrade` refusal, and the upgrade is forwarded; drop the method bound, and the `POST` is forwarded; forward the page's own path, and `/api` arrives as `/api`. Positive control: `/assets/app.js` arrives as `/_public/assets/app.js`.
      - *A page reaches nothing but `/_public/`.* Against the Worker's port, on the dev Star's host with a built app, each defence in its own limb, recognised by the answer it gives. A page load of `/api` gets the built app's page, never the build backend's own answer, which is a 400 to a request that is not an upgrade. A WebSocket upgrade to `/api` gets the helper's refusal, matched by its message. A page load carrying `x-lumenize-do-instance-name-or-id: evil.x` on `dev.never-made.acme.lumenize.localhost`, a galaxy never created, which the page step forwards without asking the Registry, so the load is that Galaxy's first contact in every phase, is not answered with a name-mismatch 500 and leaves the Galaxy named for itself. Mutations, one per limb: forward the page's own path, and the page load gets the backend's 400; drop the `Upgrade` refusal, and the helper's message is missing; let the helper copy the client's headers rather than set its own, and the Galaxy is named `evil.x`. Positive control: the dev tab loads its app.
      - *The audit holds.* `npm run audit:do-http` passes, and fails when a `.fetch(` on a Durable Object stub is added outside the named forwards, when a mesh node's `onRequest` dispatches a prefix it did not register, when a `routeDORequest` call under `apps/` passes no `bindings`, or when a method is called on a Durable Object stub outside `rawRpcStub` and the named forwards. Mutations, one per check: add the stray `.fetch(`, add the unregistered prefix, delete `bindings` at Nebula's call site, and add an inline `getByName(…).teardown()`, and each fails it.
      - *No client-supplied scope reaches the server.* A page sends the refresh a body of `{ activeScope: <a sibling> }`, and the minted `aud` is still its host's. Mutation: read the body, and the sibling's scope comes back.
      - *No scope-host page reads Home's data.* A credentialed request from a scope-host page to `home-summary` and to `pending-membership` is refused by the same-origin rule, matched by its message, and its response carries no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`. Mutations, one per limb: relax the rule to refuse only `cross-site`, and the request reaches the handler; answer CORS through the router-wide wrapper again, and the header appears. Positive control: Home, same-origin, reads it.
      - *`/auth/` answers only on the platform host.* On the dev Star's host with a built app, whose single-page fallback would otherwise answer 200, and on a universe host, a `POST` to `/auth/claim-universe` and a `GET` of `/auth/login` answer 404. Mutation: dispatch `/auth/*` by path on every host, and the `POST` reaches the claim. Positive control: the same `POST` on the platform host reaches `turnstileGuard`.
      - *A client with no configured scope finds its own.* A client constructed with no scope subscribes to its host's Star once its first token arrives. Mutation: subscribe before the first refresh, and the subscription names no Star.
      - *A session survives its token lapsing through the new refresh.* In `/live`, with a real wait past `ACCESS_TOKEN_TTL`, fifteen minutes, since nothing short of a real lapse proves a session survives one (`calibration.md` §13): a Browser-shim client holding a real login's cookies makes a call, waits out its token with its socket open, and its next call re-refreshes at the platform host with `Origin` and cookies and lands. Mutation: reuse the old token after expiry, and the Gateway refuses the call.
      - *Home's summary reads a bounded number of cookies, and the logout ends every one.* In `/live`, a Node `fetch` carrying a hundred forged `__Host-refresh-token.*` names, with a per-hash marker filtered by each request's id, with the presence guard. Home's summary resolves at most 2 × `MINT_ALL_COOKIE_CAP` hashes, and a name that parses as no scope is dropped unread. The logout's response expires all hundred names and logs one truncation warning. Mutations, one per route: resolve every cookie in the summary, and its markers pass the bound; expire only the bounded set in the logout, and a name is left unexpired.
      - *Every lane passes.* `npm test` in `apps/nebula` and `packages/nebula-auth`, plus `npx vitest run --project chromium` and `npx vitest run --project ui-smoke` in `apps/nebula`, which `npm test` does not run, are green; `npm run test:doc`'s checker is green; and `drive.ts all` sweeps green, each re-grounded limb's mutation checked per limb (`live.md`).
      - *A signup that names an account and its first app ends in Studio.* A `/live` scenario posts the claim for `acme` and `crm` through vite's port with a real email, follows the link as sent, accepts, and lands on `crm.acme.lumenize.localhost` through the claim record's `return_to`. Once Phase 15 adds the count-up, it ends at once locally. It posts over HTTP; `signup-to-first-app`'s first limb covers the rendered form.
    - **Guidance owned:**
      - **Rules and skills.** `coding-style.md`'s `*Guard` clause, whose promised return of `passageGuard` and `dominionOverScopeGuard` cannot arrive once no scoped route survives. `critical.md`'s `/auth/{scope}/magic-link?token=…` example of a query-string secret. `security.md`'s refresh-cookie description; its derived-session rule, whose cookie-path argument is void under `Path=/` while `#mintedFrom` remains the control; its only worked case for an `act`-presence check, `forwardWithSubject`, which this phase deletes; "Cookie or token, never both" as a rule; and its rationale for the refresh's Registry fallback, which now expires a cookie on a double miss and runs on the platform host. `mesh.md` § *Node identity is stamped on every first-contact entry*'s sentence on where the `fetch` path's identity headers come from, which becomes the page helper. `.claude/skills/live/SKILL.md`'s prod-drive section. `testing.md` § *Real-browser tests*, re-derived for scope hosts over `http` with `Lax` cookies.
      - **The tracks rule's neighbours.** `raw-comm.md` § *Edge Worker fronting a DO*, which shows the `forwardWith…` steps and the scoped instance paths, and its header, stated by property: `apps/nebula`'s business logic reaches a node only over the mesh or through `@rawRpc`, and its Worker reaches a Durable Object's `fetch` only through the forwards `audit:do-http` lists. `workers-projects.md`'s `apps/nebula` row, stated the same way. `raw-comm.md`'s `paths:` widen to `apps/nebula/src/**`, where the page helper and `onRequest` live, with `CLAUDE.md`'s index row.
      - **`mesh.md`'s `paths:`, re-checked.** The lookup and the consuming `POST` call the `Profile` through `rawRpcStub`, so `git grep -ln '@lumenize/mesh/raw-rpc' -- packages/nebula-auth/src` is re-run here and any new importer is added by name, the way Phase 7 added the first. A `packages/nebula-auth/src/**` glob would load `mesh.md`'s ban on raw primitives onto the Registry's own raw layer, which is raw by design.
      - **Published docs.** `website/docs/nebula/nebula-client.md` and `using-vue.md`'s client-config clauses, `auth-flows.md`'s session flow — the refresh, `scope-summary`, the cookie paths and its `accept-invite` clauses — and `api-reference.md`'s client-config section with its two `@check-example` blocks, which check the scaffold's `nebula.ts` and `for-docs.test.ts`, and `website/docs/routing/cors-support.mdx`'s block on `router.ts`'s CORS code, with the embedded copy in `platform-embed.ts` regenerated.
      - **ADRs and `auth.md`.** The ADR-021 and ADR-022 clauses about one host, the cookie path and the client naming scopes, ADR-022's clause on the consume running on a link's `GET`, ADR-021's clause on the preview running on Studio's own origin, and ADR-007's note on the `/_public/` prefix, with `auth.md`'s matching notes, its § *Home* note and its § *Impersonation* note on the `act` refusal.
      - **Source comments.** `consumeAndLogin`'s placement JSDoc, which named a page that `POST`s on a click as the remedy if a consume ever first-touched a per-user object, and which this phase adopts, so the first touch comes from the person's browser rather than a scanner. `handleAcceptMembership`'s "the ONE writer of acceptance" and `router.ts`'s matching comment, which become one of two writers sharing the Registry's acceptance method. Every comment calling a link reusable within its TTL, or scanner-safe for that reason, which `consumeAndLogin`'s placement JSDoc, `resolveConsume`'s, and the magic-link and invite-token types and schemas do today, and which disagree with each other: `schemas.ts` calls an invite token single-use where `types.ts` calls it reusable.

11. **Every action lives on the page that owns it** (§ *Home reads by cookie, and every action runs where it can*). Home groups by Profile, marks the rows needing a fresh login, links each action to the host that can perform it, and fast-forwards someone who can work in one place. The platform host's logout page offers ending every device, and the Profile page links to it with that preselected. The universe page keeps its list of apps, read through the facade, opens its create form from `?create`, navigates to `?create` itself only when the account has none, and carries the data-use notice the Manage panel did. The universe page gains "Delete this account", and Studio "Delete this app", each behind a confirmation that never rides a URL; Studio also lists the galaxy's tenant Stars, with a delete for each. The avatar menu's Home and Email addresses links point at the platform host.
    - **Success criteria (capable of failing):**
      - *Home opens from a visit that names no scope, and shows every Profile the browser holds.* A `/live` browser scenario logs in two addresses holding memberships at **disjoint** scopes, asserts both cookie names are on the request, then opens `platform.lumenize.localhost` in a fresh tab and sees both groups, with the rows needing a fresh login marked. Mutation: read only the first cookie, and the second group is missing.
      - *Home offers nothing but picking, consent and logging out.* Its page sends no request but `home-summary`, the consent pair and the logout. Mutation: give it an acting route, and the check reds.
      - *Logging out everywhere ends a session on a device this browser never saw.* Two browser contexts hold sessions for one address; in the first, "Log out" reaches the logout page, whose everywhere option is taken; the second's next refresh 401s. Mutation: end only the asking browser's cookies, and the second stays signed in. Its record names each `sub` whose sessions ended, filtered by the logout's id with the presence guard. Mutation: batch the hashes with no subject, and the line names none.
      - *The universe page lists its apps, and the create form opens itself only when there are none.* ui-smoke opens a universe page holding one app and sees the list, not the modal, and Home's "+ App" for that account opens the form. Mutations: drop the list read, and the modal opens over an account that has apps; gate `?create` on emptiness, and Home's link opens nothing. Second limb: deleting the account's only app opens the form. Mutation: open it only on a first load, and it stays shut.
      - *Creating and deleting an app work from the pages that offer them.* ui-smoke deletes an app, created on the universe page's form as Phase 10 retargeted, from Studio's "Delete this app" as a galaxy admin who holds no membership at the universe. Mutation: move "Delete this app" to the universe page, and that admin cannot reach it. `delete-scope.test.ts`'s two skipped tests retarget here.
      - *Studio lists the galaxy's tenant Stars and deletes one*, and a caller without dominion over the galaxy is refused, matched by message. Mutation: drop the facade's pre-check, and the refusal comes from the Registry instead.
      - *An account is deleted from its universe page.* ui-smoke deletes an account holding two apps from the universe page, and Home no longer lists the account or either app. Mutation: offer the delete nowhere, and Home's account Delete lands on a page with none. The teardown behind the button is Phase 7's `/live` limb, which deletes a universe through the facade and reads one marker per target, since ui-smoke captures no stack output.
      - *Home fast-forwards someone who can work in one place.* A person whose one membership is a universe holding one app lands in that app's Studio from Home. Mutation: open the universe page instead, and they land there.
    - **Guidance owned:** `.claude/rules/ui-routing.md`, whose `nebula.returnTo` and path-segment scope describe code Phases 10 and 11 replace.

12. **Passage and dominion read the host's scope** (§ *Passage and dominion read the host's scope*). `hasDominionOver` and `hasPassageInto` take the verified claims and read `aud` in `authScope`'s place. A plain membership's token verifies only when its `aud` equals its `authScope`, and the mint builds only such a token, so `buildNebulaJwtPayload`'s claim to mirror verification stays true. The facade's invite eligibility compares `aud`, and the Registry's own checks — `createGalaxy`'s, `planScopeDeletion`'s and `#computeDeletionPlan`'s — read the claims the facade hands them through the predicate. `profile.ts`'s super-admin short-circuit narrows to `NEBULA_SUB`. `requireDominionHere`'s refusal names the host's scope, `requirePassage`'s says passage and names both scopes, the reserved platform name gets a refusal of its own, and `dominionOverHostAtSubscribe`'s JSDoc derives from the new predicate. `parse-id.ts`'s allow-list JSDoc is conformed to § *Claims the build rests on*.
    - **Success criteria (capable of failing):**
      - *Dominion is contained by the page.* A universe admin's token from a tenant's page is refused dominion over a sibling tenant, over the galaxy and over the universe, each matched by its message naming the host's scope; the same person's token from the universe page holds all three. Mutation: read `authScope` again, and all three pass.
      - *A superuser's Profile access is bounded by the page.* A superuser's token from a tenant's page is refused private read on a Profile whose memberships all sit in another universe; from that universe's page it is allowed. Mutation: keep the `authScope` short-circuit, and the tenant-page read passes. Second limb: `NEBULA_SUB`'s Profile stays editable by a superuser. Mutation: delete its short-circuit, and the edit is refused with `#requireOwnerOrAdmin`'s message.
      - *The upward arm is bounded by the callee, not by the barrier.* For a token whose `aud` is the dev Star, held once by a galaxy admin and once by a universe admin — the callers the host rule creates, since a plain Star member is refused either way — derive the set it can call on the Galaxy and on the Universe — every `@mesh()` entry on each host's whole prototype chain through `meshEntries` in `apps/nebula/test/test-apps/mesh-surface.ts`; every member of the `resources` door and of the `OrgTree` it hands out, whose reasons `scope-isolation.test.ts`'s tables give; and every member of `resourcesResults`, each asserted refused on the request leg — and assert each is **refused, bounded by a grant, or recorded as deliberately open with its reason**. `grep -nE '^\s*@mesh\(' apps/nebula/src/galaxy.ts apps/nebula/src/universe.ts` returned 11 decorated members on 2026-09-30, which only starts the first part. Name `resources.invite` explicitly, since its bound is the facade's. Positive control: `resources.transaction` is callable at all; every refusal matched by message, per limb. ADR-015 § *Deliberately open* asks for this sweep.
      - *An admin working from a lower page is held to grants above it.* The same two holders are refused `requireChatWrite`'s members on the Galaxy with the DAG message rather than the dominion message. Mutation: the predicates read `authScope`, and `writeSource` succeeds.
      - *A plain membership's token whose `aud` sits below its `authScope` fails verification.* Witnessed in-lane by `scope-verification.test.ts`'s `craftJwt`, a rung-4 mint with settable `aud` and `authScope`, since no real mint path can produce one after this phase and the Gateway collapses every refusal to one message. Mutation: drop the equality, and it verifies; let the mint build one, and the mint-side check passes it. Positive control: its `aud == authScope` case verifies.
      - *The Registry's own check follows the host rule.* In-lane, since no running system reaches the Registry but through the facade, which refuses first: calling the Registry's `createGalaxy` or `executeScopeDeletion` directly with a universe admin's claims from a tenant's page is refused. Mutation: derive `aud` from `authScope` inside the Registry, and it is admitted.
      - *The facade refuses below the target's parent.* In `/live`, a universe admin's token from `dev.crm.acme` calling `executeScopeDeletion('acme.crm')` or `createGalaxy('acme.x')` is refused with the facade's message, and the same admin on a tenant's page inviting into the galaxy is refused with a message naming the host's scope. Mutations, one per limb: skip the facade's pre-check, or have it read `authScope`, and the refusal comes from the Registry or the call succeeds.
      - *Fixtures whose verdict flips are found by property.* Every `universeAdminClient` and `adminClientAt` call whose `activeScope` sits below the node it acts on with dominion is re-grounded at that node's scope, or asserts the refusal; `universeAdminClient`'s JSDoc, which promises cross-tier reach, is conformed. The suites passing is the instrument.
      - *A refusal names the operand that decided it.* A universe admin refused over the galaxy from a tenant page is told the host's scope, where today's message would say "your admin scope is acme". Mutation: restore the `authScope` message. `grep -rn "admin scope is\|Admin access required for\|Token scope" apps packages --include='*.ts'` finds the assertions on that wording, the facade's invite refusal and Phase 7's pre-check messages among the sources, `four-party-chat.ts`'s whole-string `assert.equal` and `dev-studio.test.ts`'s substrings among them, and each matches the new message.
      - *A passage refusal says passage and names both scopes, and the platform name is refused in words of its own.* A tenant-page token calling a sibling tenant is refused with a message naming passage, the target and the host's scope; a superuser's call to the platform name is refused with a different one. `grep -rln 'Active-scope mismatch' apps packages --include='*.ts'` returned 10 files on 2026-09-30, the source sites, six baseline test files and the `passage-not-dominion` scenario among them, and returns nothing afterwards; `four-party-chat`'s and `source-chat-floor.test.ts`'s hits are comments. Mutation: restore `'Active-scope mismatch'`, and the new assertions red.
      - *One admin's sibling tab is refused at the Gateway.* `client-gateway-passage.test.ts`'s skipped case *one galaxy admin's tab on a sibling Star is refused, though their scope covers both*, skipped at Phase 2, is un-skipped and green: a galaxy admin's tab on `acme.crm.b` refuses a call from `acme.crm.a`, matched by the passage message. Mutation: hand the predicate `access` instead of the claims, and it passes again by dominion.
      - *Every verdict reads `aud` through a predicate.* Stated as a property, the way `parse-id.ts`'s allow-list is, because this build adds readers in several phases: `grep -rnE '\.aud\b|\baud:' apps/nebula/src packages/nebula-auth/src --include='*.ts' | grep -v platform-embed | grep -vE ':[0-9]+: *(\*|//)'` returned six lines in four files on 2026-09-29, and every hit afterwards is a verdict through `hasDominionOver` or `hasPassageInto`, or is read hit by hit and classed as a message, a record, a construction, or a client reading its own token. Phase 13 re-runs it for the opener record it adds. Mutation: decide a verdict from `aud` directly at a call site, and the read classes it as none of those.
      - *`parse-id.ts`'s own grep returns exactly the new exception set.* Mutation: add an `isAtOrAbove` call outside it, and the grep returns it.
    - **Guidance owned:** `.claude/rules/security.md`'s scope rule, and delegation rule (2)'s `aud` clause and its theorem, with `canMintFor`'s JSDoc. `website/docs/nebula/auth-flows.md`'s Layer-4 "Admin access required" message. `website/docs/nebula/coding-your-ui.md`'s paragraph on the claims, which glosses `aud` as an audience and `access` as the user's scope grant and cites an aud-lock, and `api-reference.md`'s paragraph on `access`, with `platform-embed.ts` regenerated. `.claude/rules/calibration.md` §14, whose example this phase reverses for a reason it does not cover — the page's code rather than the person. ADR-015's and ADR-022's clause saying the checks read `authScope`, and `auth.md`'s.

13. **A persona's host mints the persona's token, and nothing else can** (§ *Acceptance runs on the platform host*). The refresh answers a page on `manny--dev.crm.acme.lumenize.dev` with Manny's plain token when the browser holds a cookie whose ACCEPTED membership has dominion over `acme.crm.dev`, judged from that cookie's record and never its name, and 401s otherwise; it does so only in the `dev` Star (ADR-022 § *A persona's host*), and Phase 9's parse already refuses a persona under a tenant. The branch reuses Phase 10's candidate selection, requiring an accepted `scopeAdmin` record whose own scope sits at or above the Star, so it adds no `isAtOrAbove` call of its own and Phase 12's allow-list stays exact. It records the opener: Manny's `sub`, the Star, the page's `aud`, and the opener's `sub`, `profileId`, scope and `scopeAdmin` from the cookie's Workers KV record, with no acting token (ADR-016). Manny's `sub` and `profileId` are one version-5 UUID, computed from a fixed Lumenize namespace UUID and his name in his Star, `acme.crm.dev/manny` (§ *Decisions*).
    - **Success criteria (capable of failing):**
      - *The persona gets its own token, and only for dominion.* One `/live` scenario: a dev Star admin's browser gets Manny's plain token on his host — no `act`, no `scopeAdmin`, `authScope` and `aud` both `acme.crm.dev` — and an admin-invited collaborator, whose co-minted `.dev` membership carries `scopeAdmin`, gets it too. Mutation: treat the host as its Star, and the page gets its opener's own token instead.
      - *Nobody else gets Manny, each refused as the refresh's own 401.* A peer-invited plain member of `acme.crm.dev`; an admin-invited collaborator at `acme.crm`, whose co-minted `.dev` membership would give dominion over `acme.crm.dev` once accepted, signed in through a plain magic link and never accepted, since closing the consent screen leaves no cookie; and a galaxy admin on `manny--staging.crm.acme`. Mutations, one per limb: fall through to the ordinary selection, and the plain member gets their own token; drop the accepted conjunct, and the unaccepted invitee gets Manny's; drop the `dev` check, and the staging persona mints. Positive control: the unaccepted invitee, after Accept, gets Manny's token.
      - *The opener is recorded.* After the persona refresh, the stack's log names both the opener's `sub` and Manny's, filtered by the refresh's id with the presence guard. Mutation: drop the record, and no line names the opener.
      - *The opener is judged by the cookie's record, not its name.* After a real login as the admin of a universe `evil`, a Node `fetch` sends that admin's own refresh value as `__Host-refresh-token.acme`, with `Origin` naming `manny--dev.crm.acme.lumenize.localhost`, and gets 401, matched as the refresh's own. Mutation: decide the cookie's position by its name, and Manny's token is minted. Positive control: the first limb's dev Star admin, under the true name.
      - *The persona's id is a name-based GUID that no person's can equal.* The minted `sub` equals the `profileId`, carries 5 as its version digit, and is the same across two refreshes in two browsers. Mutation: mint with `crypto.randomUUID()`, and the two refreshes disagree and the version digit is 4. Positive control: the persona's tab opens a socket and makes a call, so the Gateway accepts the id.
    - **Re-run Phase 12's `aud` sweep**, since the opener record adds a reader; it is classed as a record.
    - **Guidance owned:** `website/docs/nebula/auth-flows.md`'s session flow, which Phase 10 rewrote, gains the persona branch, with `platform-embed.ts` regenerated. `security.md`'s derived-session rule gains the persona token with the personas build, which owns the persona tab's `logout()` that rule would have to govern.

14. **ui-smoke and the impersonation scenarios pass on Linux.** CI runs only on pull requests and pushes to `main`, so the Linux run is a draft pull request from `pre-alpha` to `main`, as `ci.yml`'s header recommends for a long-lived branch; opening it is outward-facing and waits for Larry's go. It runs `ui-smoke.yml` and `ci.yml`'s `run-live-scenarios` on `ubuntu-latest`, which exercise the resolver mapping that stands in for the `*.lumenize.dev` record on Linux. `run-live-scenarios` runs the two impersonation scenarios, which are Node-only, so Chromium's half rides ui-smoke.
    - **Success criteria (capable of failing):**
      - *Both are green on `ubuntu-latest`.* Mutations, measured first since Chromium may resolve `*.localhost` on its own: drop the `lookup` hook, and Node's hosts answer `ENOTFOUND`; drop `--host-resolver-rules`, and ui-smoke's first page load fails, or the criterion records that Chromium needs no rule there.
    - **Guidance owned:** `.claude/skills/live/SKILL.md`'s scenario notes.

15. **A galaxy's certificate is ordered where it is created, and the person waits where they already are** (§ *Certificates and the wait*). The Galaxy's Durable Object orders its pack, polls it and retries from its own alarm through `svc.alarms.schedule(…)` — a wake only records that a pack is wanted and arms the alarm, so the alarm handler is the one caller of Cloudflare's API and two wakes cannot order twice — keeping the pack's id and status in its own storage, and its teardown deletes every pack naming its host, found by hostname, before it wipes (§ *Certificates and the wait*).
   - **What wakes it.** nebula-auth's `orderCertificate` hook wakes it, after the creation's teardown has returned, after a `createGalaxy` that succeeded, and after either acceptance writer, the link page's `POST` or Home's `accept-membership`, gets back the galaxies beneath a claimed universe from the Registry's acceptance method, accepted or already accepted. Ordering is idempotent, and a Worker that died before the hook leaves nothing else to retry it, which is why already accepted orders too; the Registry names only galaxies whose `Scopes` rows stand.
   - **A wake a deletion overtakes is reaped.** After each wake, the caller re-reads that galaxy's `Scopes` row and, if it is gone, calls `teardown` on the galaxy again, which disarms it and deletes any pack naming its host. Without it, a deletion landing between the Registry's answer and the wake leaves a fresh object, its tearing-down flag lost with the abort, ordering a pack for a galaxy with no row, outside the owner cap and past every later teardown. It is the write-then-reap shape the refresh records use, at one indexed Registry read per wake. Both writers fire it from their one Worker helper; `apps/nebula` answers with a raw RPC call to `orderCertificate()`, and `onStart` orders nothing (§ *Which host answers what*).
   - **An order in flight cannot outlive its galaxy.** Teardown's `ctx.abort()` would kill an alarm handler still waiting on Cloudflare's API, and `deleteAll()` has already cleared its alarm, so nothing in the Galaxy could clean up after it. Teardown's first act, before any `await`, sets a tearing-down flag that the wake and the alarm handler both check before calling Cloudflare's API, and cancels the pending alarm and the wanted state, since every `await` after it opens the input gate to an alarm that would otherwise start an order the listing misses. It then waits, with a bound, for an order already in flight, holding the order's promise in an in-memory latch as `containers.md` describes for lifecycle transitions, then lists the packs naming the galaxy's host through the API and deletes each. An order that outlives the wait is logged at error with its hostname, for the sweep ADR-021 names. The pack calls in teardown run outside `blockConcurrencyWhile`, each with a timeout of its own.
   - **Home's fast-forward waits too.** A ticket-backed claim is accepted on Home, so Home's fast-forward to the new app shows the same count-up and polls the same destination host the signup page does. Only an `https` origin orders, and an `https` deployment without `CERTIFICATE_API_TOKEN` fails its preflight. Teardown's pack step runs only where ordering does, so on an `http` origin teardown is the wipe alone.
   - **A pack matches by its galaxy's names, never by any host it holds.** Every pack names the apex `lumenize.dev`, so matching on any host would delete every customer's pack, and a bare suffix would match `xcrm.acme`'s pack for `crm.acme`. A pack belongs to the galaxy when it names the galaxy's host or its wildcard exactly, and teardown deletes no other. The universe page and the platform host show a seconds count-up and poll the destination host. The deployed venue names what it creates `test-{MMDD}-{random}` and sweeps old packs.
    - **Before its deployed criteria run, Larry sets up the test zone** (§ *Constraints*), since each step is outward-facing.
    - **Success criteria (capable of failing):**
      - *The ordering state machine is right in-lane.* It is a pure function of the stored state and the last API result to the next action and the next alarm, so pool-workers is right for it. A stored pack id means a second wake orders nothing, and a wake while an order is in flight orders nothing either; a transient failure schedules a retry; a pending status re-arms the alarm; an active status stops. Mutation for each: order again on a stored id, and a second wake double-orders; order from the wake directly, and two wakes double-order; drop the retry, and a failure is terminal; stop re-arming on pending, and the pack is never seen active; keep polling once active, and the alarm never stops.
      - *Teardown is a state of the same machine.* Its first step is always `disarm`: with no order in flight its output is `[disarm, deletePacksNamingHost, wipe]`, with one in flight `[disarm, awaitOrder, deletePacksNamingHost, wipe]`, and on an `http` origin `[disarm, wipe]`; an order that outlives the bound adds a log naming the hostname, and a failed delete still yields `wipe` and logs the id and hostname. A wake or an alarm arriving after `disarm` orders nothing. Mutations, one per limb: wipe first, and no pack is listed; delete by the stored id, and a pack whose order returned during teardown is missed; skip the wait, and the list runs before the in-flight order returns; disarm after the list, and a wake mid-teardown orders; list packs on `http`, and the local teardown calls the API; stop on the failure, and the storage survives.
      - *Teardown deletes only its galaxy's packs.* In-lane, since it is a pure predicate over a pack's hosts: a pack naming `crm.acme.lumenize.dev` or `*.crm.acme.lumenize.dev` matches, and an apex-only pack, `xcrm.acme.lumenize.dev`'s and `crm.acme2.lumenize.dev`'s do not. Mutations, one per limb: match any host in the pack, and the apex-only pack matches; match by bare suffix, and `xcrm.acme`'s matches.
      - *The wake reaches the Galaxy by name, and only from our own code.* Locally, an acceptance through the link page, a ticket-backed claim accepted from Home, and a facade create each fire the Galaxy's `orderCertificate` marker, which carries `this.lmz.instanceName` read with no `ctx.id.name` fallback, and the `http` origin then orders nothing. A universe admin's mesh call to `acme.never-created` never fires it. Mutations, one per limb: stamp no identity on the internal call, and the marker carries no instance name; fire the hook only from the link page's `POST`, and the Home acceptance is silent; order from `onStart`, and the never-created Galaxy fires it.
      - *A wake a deletion overtakes is torn down.* In nebula-auth's lane, since no running system can time a deletion into that gap, the recording stub's `orderCertificate` deletes the galaxy's `Scopes` row through `runInDurableObject` when it is called. A `createGalaxy` through the facade, and an acceptance through the helper, each then record a `teardown` call for that galaxy after the order. Mutation, per caller: drop the re-read, and no teardown is recorded. Positive control: with the row left standing, none is.
      - *A refused create or acceptance orders nothing.* An owner at the cap whose `createGalaxy`, link-page Accept and Home Accept are each refused, each matched by the cap's message, fires no `orderCertificate` marker carrying those calls' ids. Mutations, one per site: call the hook at `createGalaxy` whatever the Registry answered; call it from the acceptance helper whatever the outcome; each fires the marker. Positive controls: below the cap, each site fires it. A second limb, on a universe with one galaxy: accepting the claim's membership again from Home after that galaxy is deleted, the one path left that repeats an acceptance once the link is spent, fires no marker carrying its id. Mutation: take the claim's galaxy from the spent link's `returnTo`, and it fires.
      - *A missing token fails the preflight.* An `https` deploy config without `CERTIFICATE_API_TOKEN` exits non-zero naming it. Mutation: drop it from the preflight's required list, and the deploy proceeds.
      - *Creating an app waits for Studio's host, then opens it.* On the deployed target, Galaxy create shows the count-up and enters Studio once the new host answers, and the as-you dev tab loads. Mutation: order no pack, and Studio's host never answers.
      - *A signup waits where it can and arrives when it can.* On the deployed target, the signup stops at the platform host's count-up and enters Studio only once that host's handshake succeeds. Mutation: navigate as soon as consent returns, and the run meets the certificate error. A ticket-backed signup, accepted on Home, waits on Home's count-up the same way. Mutation: fast-forward at once, and the run meets the certificate error.
      - *Deleting a galaxy deletes its pack.* On the deployed target, a galaxy deleted at once after its create, while its order may still be in flight, leaves no pack naming its host, and so does a create and a delete of one galaxy fired concurrently, checked after waiting past the alarm, and a universe deleted with two galaxies leaves neither pack listed. A deleted active pack stays listed as `pending_deletion` for ten minutes and more, so a pack on its way out counts as gone. Positive control: a sibling galaxy's pack and the zone's Universal pack are still listed after each delete. Mutation: skip the delete in teardown, and the packs are still listed.
      - *The harness sweep deletes only the packs of `test-` hosts older than its window.* Mutation: drop the age check, and a concurrent run's pack goes.

16. **The deployed pass, and the guidance catches up.** The registry runs against `lumenize-test.dev` with `HARNESS_TARGET_URL`, covering the class only a deployment shows: `run_worker_first` on real hosts, real TLS and certificate packs, and Chromium on a real `.dev` site. The docs no earlier phase owned are conformed.
    - **Before the deployed sweep, a run's scenarios share one app** (§ *Certificates and the wait*; Larry chose sharing plus cleanup on 2026-10-03, against the costs in § *Decisions*). `sharedApp(stack)` claims a universe and its first app through the real signup the first time a run asks, names both `test-{MMDD}-…`, and hands every later ask in that run the same names and owner address; it keys on the stack's id and the run's id, so a local boot, with storage of its own, always makes its own. A scenario that shares founds tenant Stars of its own beneath the shared app; one that must own its universe names it with `testSlug` and deletes what it claimed at its end. Node's connections into a scope's host wait for the host to answer, as `HostWait` does in the browser, so a deployed scenario waits out a new app's certificate rather than failing its TLS handshake.
    - **Success criteria (capable of failing):**
      - *A run's scenarios share one app, and a boot never inherits another's.* Two asks under one stack and run return one app; a new stack id makes another. Mutation: key on the run alone, and the second boot is handed a universe its fresh storage never held.
      - *What a scenario claims, it deletes.* Every scenario that owns its universe deletes it at its end, pass or fail. On the deployed target, the zone's advanced-pack count after a sweep is the shared app's plus any a failed scenario left, each named, and the sweep reports its peak. Mutation: skip the cleanup, and the count after the sweep grows by every owning scenario.
      - *A deployed scenario waits for its app's host.* On the deployed target, a scenario connecting into a new app's host waits for the handshake to succeed; locally the host answers at once. Mutation: skip the wait, and the connection fails its TLS handshake.
      - *A page load on every host reaches the Worker.* On the deployed target a Star host serves its built app. Mutation: restore the path list, and it serves Studio's `index.html`.
      - *A deployed page reaches nothing but `/_public/`.* On the deployed target, on the dev Star's host, a page load of `/api` gets the built app's page, and a WebSocket upgrade to `/api` gets the helper's refusal, matched by its message. Mutations, one per limb: forward the page's own path, and the page load gets the build backend's 400; drop the `Upgrade` refusal, and the message is missing.
      - *A deleted scope's storage is gone on Cloudflare.* On the deployed target, after an admin deletes an app and before anything re-creates it, Cloudflare's API reports no stored data for the id of each Durable Object the app held.
        - **How the harness learns the ids.** Node cannot compute `idFromName`, so the harness tails the Worker while it creates the app, and each object's id is the `durableObjectId` of the event that logs the app's own creation teardown. Cloudflare's object listing trails the objects by six to eight minutes, so the limb waits for the listing in both directions; it reads the listing with the read token § *Constraints* adds to Larry's setup.
        - **Why the API, and why before a re-create.** Any call to the object would stamp its identity and write storage again, and a re-create's own teardown would empty it either way.
        - **The deletion logs no teardown error**, confirming the rejection Phase 7 measured locally is the one Cloudflare delivers.
        - Positive controls: the listing gains the Galaxy's and the `.dev` Star's ids across the create, and before the delete the API reports stored data for the Galaxy's. Mutations, one per limb: drop `deleteAll()`, and stored data is reported; count the abort's rejection a failure, and the error is logged. Dropping the macrotask yield before `ctx.abort()` was measured on 2026-10-03 not to red: an awaited `deleteAll()` followed straight by the abort left no stored data on Cloudflare. `durable-objects.md`'s loss was measured on a synchronous `put`, so this does not contradict it, and the yield stays.
      - *Every deployed host carries its `frame-ancestors`.* On the deployed target the platform host, a universe page, Studio and the dev Star's host each answer a page load with the value Phase 10 pins, and Phase 10's two iframes of Home and Studio, added to a signed-in page on the dev Star's host, are blocked. Mutation: stamp it in vite alone, and the deployed responses lack it and the Home frame renders.
      - *The deployed sweep is green*, each limb that reads `DevStack.logs` guarded and reported as not observable there.
      - *The guidance this build owes is conformed.* Whatever of `website/docs/nebula/` the earlier phases' clauses left, last changed in Phase 15 or 16, with the bundle's embedded copy regenerated. The rows § *Backlog rows this build settles* names, each carrying its verdict. Re-run the triage grep § *Constraints* records, and read every hit.
      - *Nothing outside this build points at a section of it that no longer exists.* Every `§ *Name*` in `grep -rn 'nebula-scope-moves-to-subdomain' docs tasks .claude --include='*.md' | grep -v /archive/` resolves against a heading in this file: 48 pointer lines in 13 files on 2026-10-02. And no source names this file: `git grep -n 'nebula-scope-moves-to-subdomain' -- apps packages` returned 4 lines in 3 files on 2026-09-30, each going in an earlier phase, and returns nothing.
      - *The old host is named only where its retirement is.* `grep -rl 'nebula\.lumenize\.com' --exclude-dir=node_modules --exclude-dir=archive --exclude-dir=.git . | grep -v '^./experiments'` returned 24 files on 2026-09-30, and returns only task files, dated records such as `apps/nebula/harness/FINDINGS.md`, and the ADR and `auth.md` clauses that say it retires. `packages/nebula-auth/README.md` and `RELEASING.md` are among those it corrects.
      - *No "Today's code differs" clause this build closes survives.* `grep -n '^> \*\*Today' docs/vision/auth.md` and the same over ADR-007, 015, 021, 022 and 023 return only clauses naming work outside this build, each read. On 2026-10-02 that is ADR-021's persona slug floor and production's DNS at the wipe, the platform-name reservation in ADR-015 and `auth.md`, and `auth.md`'s notes on the Universe layer, a second address on a Profile, history records and the climb.

## Non-goals

- **The signup and login pages' display names, host preview and design.** The shared-pages item in [nebula-pre-alpha.md](nebula-pre-alpha.md) owns them; this build owns the claim behind them, and the first app's field it adds to both forms.
- **A custom login page on an app's own origin**, and a customer's own domain. § *Future state* holds both.
- **The persona cast, its provisioning, its records, the tab strip, seeding and reaping.** [nebula-testing-with-personas.md](nebula-testing-with-personas.md).
- **Sweeping the packs a failed teardown left behind.** [backlog.md](backlog.md) § *Infrastructure*, in the row on watching the zone's galaxy count.
- **HTTP to a scope's Durable Objects.** After pre-alpha, per ADR-022 § *Deliberately open*.
- **A tenant founder's own pages.** A backlog row, until real tenants exist (§ *Decisions*).
- **Production's DNS, keys and certificate token.** The wipe's list in [nebula-pre-alpha.md](nebula-pre-alpha.md) § *⑥ The wipe*.
