The 401 runs its `window.location.assign('/login')`, which replaces the frame's document with the Studio's login page, so limb 6 passes only when its wait sees the built app's `<base href>` before that navigation lands. Both runs alone showed the same 401, and one of them failed. Phase 10 removes the `/app/` path and the meta's scope fields, and limb 6 then waits for the bootstrap's refresh to settle before reading the frame, so the race cannot hide a refusal again.
- **Phase 8's cap is 20** (Larry, 2026-10-02). `MAX_GALAXIES_PER_OWNER` and its refusal, *One account owner may hold at most 20 apps. Ask us and we will raise it.*, live in nebula-auth's `types.ts` and are re-exported through `/claims` for the harness.
- **The first app's slug is `appSlug` on both claim bodies**, refused as `invalid_app_slug`, *Invalid app slug format*, at both claim paths. A claim writes its galaxy and `.dev` Star in the universe's transaction through `#insertFirstApp`.
- **The Worker writes every refresh record a consume, a ticket claim, an acceptance or the refresh fallback puts**, through `putRefreshRecords` in `worker-token.ts`, which re-reads `getRefreshRecord` after each put and deletes the put unless `sameRefreshRecord` holds. `getRefreshRecord` writes nothing now, so the cookie routes that resolve a credential with it no longer heal Workers KV as a side effect; only the refresh does.
- **A tree level's read had counted grandchildren against its `LIMIT`.** `#childLevel` filtered direct children in JavaScript after a `LIMIT budget + 1` read of the whole prefix, so a universe whose apps each carry a `.dev` Star spent half the limit on Stars and reported no frontier past about 25 apps. Every galaxy has carried its `.dev` Star since the collapse; the claim's first app is what made `scope-summary.test.ts`'s paging test see it. `#levelPattern` now bounds the depth in SQL, for `#directChildCount` too.
- **Five test files founded dozens of universes under one address and met the cap**: `ttl-seconds` and `impersonation-mint` in nebula-auth, `org-tree`, `star-resources` and `subscriptions-table` in `apps/nebula`. Each now derives its founder's address from its universe. `identity-mint-point.test.ts` founds 14 under `scope-admin@example.com` and stays below the cap.
- **`issueInvites` checks its invariant breach before the pending-scope refusal**, since a caller bug should be loud whatever the target.
- **An account is empty only once its last app is deleted**, since signup now writes the first one. `studio-overlays-by-url` limb 2 deletes the claim's app through the person's own session, as the client's Manage panel does, then arrives at `?create` with the form open (Larry, 2026-10-02: cover it). Mutation: never auto-open, and limb 2 reds.
- **Five of Phase 8's criteria run in-lane**, and `claim-and-acceptance.test.ts`'s header says why for each: the three rows, the slug refusals, acceptance's outcomes, the stranger and superuser counts, and the reap. The superuser count also has a `/live` limb, `galaxy-cap` limb 4.
- **`harness/lib/stdio.ts` holds the debug-line parser** `scope-teardown` carried, for `galaxy-cap`, `claim-lifecycle` and `signup-to-first-app` too.
- **Two guidance updates wait for the last phase that changes them**: `website/docs/nebula/auth-flows.md`'s claim and acceptance sequences, and the claim and acceptance rows of nebula-auth's README. Phase 10 moves both routes to the platform host.
- **The mutation runner restored by match count, and two of Phase 8's specs broke it.** An empty replacement and a replacement that already occurred elsewhere each left their mutation in source, caught by the next run's guard and restored by hand. The runner now refuses both before it applies anything.
- **Phase 8's mutations, as run, each red at its own limb.** In-lane, 16: fifteen in `claim-and-acceptance.test.ts` and one on `scope-summary`'s paging test. `/live`, 4 in `galaxy-cap`, 17 in `claim-lifecycle`, 4 in `signup-to-first-app` and 1 in `studio-overlays-by-url`. Two needed a second cut. Answering before the teardown returns was first an un-awaited teardown, which died with the request as Phase 7's did; logging the completion before awaiting it reddened the ordering. And limb 2 now waits for its markers as well as its completion line, so the order is what its assertion decides.
- **A tenant Star a scenario invites into is now founded whole.** `provisionAndLogin`'s Star branch and `client-sender-passage` claimed tenants through a throwaway address that never accepted, which `issueInvites` now refuses; the first sweep reddened four scenarios on it. `foundTenantStar` in `test/lib/email-login.ts` claims, follows the founder's link as sent and accepts, on plain `fetch`, so the founder's cookie never lands in the asker's jar.
- **Phase 8's closing runs, 2026-10-02.** nebula-auth 431 and 4 skipped, the 19 new tests; `apps/nebula` 951 and 3 skipped, no test added or removed; chromium 14 and 1; ui-smoke 4 and 2; the doc checker and the type-check green. mesh and routing were not re-run, since this phase touched neither. The first sweep passed 33 of 39: four reds were the pending-tenant fixture above, `scope-teardown`'s list now holds the claim's first app, and `studio-chat-reload`'s login form lost its sent state to a second navigation to `/auth/login`, which did not recur when it ran alone.
- **Phase 8's second sweep passed 38 of 39**, after the fixture fix. The red was `first-app-built`, whose reply poll timed out reading the thinking indicator's text, 208 s into the turn, as the indicator went away; run alone, it passed all eight limbs, the reload limb included. The poll reads an element that can vanish between its count and its text, which is this scenario's own race and predates the build.
- **Phase 9's origin var is `LUMENIZE_ORIGIN`**, beside the existing `LUMENIZE_APPROVED_ORIGINS`. `hosts.ts` in nebula-auth holds `parseHost`, `platformOrigin` and `deploymentOrigin`, the last throwing when the var is absent rather than guessing, and `/claims` and `/testing` re-export all three. `parseHost` also answers the bare origin host as `apex`, and refuses a universe label the platform keeps, such as `crm.platform.lumenize.dev`.
- **`NEBULA_AUTH_ISSUER` is gone.** `buildNebulaJwtPayload` and `createNebulaTestToken` take `issuer`, the Worker's mint and verify derive it from `env`, and the facade's fallback origin is the platform host. Every test Worker config that mints or verifies names `http://lumenize.localhost`, except `apps/nebula/test/browser/worker`'s, which names `https://lumenize.localhost` for its `--local-protocol https`.
- **The deployed test target's config is `scripts/test-deploy-config.mjs`**, called by `deploy-test.sh` in place of its inline generator, and `test-deploy-config.selftest.mjs` runs in the package `test` script. `TEST_REFRESH_TOKEN_KV_ID` in the root `.dev.vars` names the namespace, and the script refuses to start without it. **No such namespace exists yet**: only production's and the browser rig's are on the account, and creating one is Phase 16's, before its deploy. Both deploy scripts self-check `/_version` on the platform host, since `workers_dev` is now `false`.
- **vite's config does not import the parse yet.** Phase 9's criterion names it, but vite has no use for the parse until Phase 10's middlewares decide by host, and an import with no use is dead code. The harness's import, which every run exercises, is the Node witness; the mutation importing from the root barrel fails it.
- **Phase 9's mutations, as run, each red at its own check**: the depth check, `parseId` in place of the slug grammar, the constant issuer, and the three config swaps the selftest guards, plus the harness importing from the root barrel.
- **Phase 9's closing runs, 2026-10-02.** nebula-auth 451 and 4 skipped, the 20 new tests; `apps/nebula` 951 and 3 skipped, with the config selftest in its `test` script; chromium 14 and 1; ui-smoke 4 and 2; the doc checker and the type-check green. The sweep passed 38 of 39: `claim-lifecycle`'s first claim was written, then its mail send through the remote email binding lost its WebSocket (`1006`) and the runtime cancelled the request as hung, answering 500. Run alone it passed all seven limbs.
- **A page learns the platform host from `<meta name="lumenize-origin">`**, which the Worker's page step and vite's two middlewares inject, at the request's own port. A built app's `nebula-scope` carries `dev` and the `parentOrigin` a framed page reports to, and nothing about its scope.
- **The platform host's routes live in `PlatformHost`, a `WorkerEntrypoint` behind the self-referencing `PLATFORM_HOST` binding, and the Worker buffers a request's body before forwarding it.** A route that answered without reading its body — Home's summary refusing a cookie-less request — left the binding pulling the stream after the answer, and workerd threw *Can't read from request stream after response has been sent* and took a local `wrangler dev` down.
- **Two vite traps.** The config imports `readFile` and `resolve` statically, since a dynamic import inside a middleware died with *module runner has been closed*. And proxy rows are tried in order with a bypass ending the search, so `/auth`, `/gateway`, `/pictures` and `/_version` come first and the host catch-all last.
- **The facade invites into the platform root again.** Phase 10 took `_platform` out of `parseId`, which the facade's tier check called, so a platform-tier invite was refused as an invalid id; the facade now checks `isPlatformScope` before it parses.
- **One browser holding an owner's cookie gets the owner's token on every page beneath the owner's scope**, since the refresh picks the broadest admin membership. Test helpers that founded a universe and a Star beneath it under one address therefore minted as the owner on the Star's page. `apps/nebula/test/test-helpers.ts` gives each universe its own owner, `ownerOf(email)`, provisioned on plain `fetch`; twenty vestigial `foundAndLogin` calls that only seeded that collision went; and the scenarios that need two people use two browsers.
- **`HARNESS_EMAIL_PROVIDER=resend` boots the local stack on Resend.** Cloudflare's daily sending quota ran out on 2026-10-03 after a day of sweeps, and every claim then answered 500 with *account daily sending quota exceeded*; the `/live` skill names the knob.
- **Mail to one much-used address arrived late, and the browser lane stopped sharing it.** On 2026-10-03, Resend's mail to `test@lumenize-test.dev` — 197 messages since 09-25 — reached the catch-all 17 to 114 s after the send, some not at all, while a fresh address arrived in about a second. The lane's smoke tests use `uniqueTestEmail()`, and its timeout is 150 s, above its two 60 s waiters, so a slow send reports as *No email received* rather than a bare test timeout.
- **The chromium lane's page sits on `tenant-a.crm.acme.lumenize.localhost`** through the project's `browser.api.host`, named again in `test/chromium/page-star.ts`. Its global setup boots Studio's vite, climbs the tree as an owner in Node, claims the Star as its own admin through vite's port, follows the emailed link in its own Chromium and clicks Accept, and writes that browser's cookies to the `storageState` the provider starts every test's context from. The `/worker` and `/dev-star` proxies, their `Origin` and `Path=` rewrites, and the package's `http-proxy` dependency went with them. Mutation: claim a Star other than the page's, and the lifecycle test reds.
- **ui-smoke boots through `bootStudioVite` and logs in with a fresh address.** The signed-out check is now the redirect to the platform host's login with Studio's URL as `return_to`, and *create an app* runs on the universe page's `?create` form in a second tab, so the codegen case's page stays put. The `EPIPE` lines in its log are vite's WebSocket proxy, logged when that tab closes with its client connected.
- **`npm run audit:do-http` parses source with TypeScript's parser and prints what it checked on every run**: 5 member `.fetch(` sites, 1 `routeDORequest` call, 1 node surface comparing 3 paths and 5 stub-making calls on 2026-10-03. A node registers its prefixes in a static `HTTP_PREFIXES`, which `Galaxy` now carries. Mutations, each failing it: a stray `.fetch(` on the Registry stub, an unregistered prefix, `bindings` removed at Nebula's call site, an inline `getByName(…).teardown()`, and an `ASSETS`-style table row naming a Durable Object binding.
- **A client with no configured scope is covered in `/live`, in `message-roundtrip`'s last limb**: a second tab constructed with no token subscribes in the same turn and gets the Message from its own host. Mutation: call at once with the scope unknown, and the subscribe is never acknowledged.
- **`refresh-reads-the-host` limb 3 now calls `setStarConfig`.** Its invite could not red under *pick the plain membership*, since a plain member may invite peers into their own scope; the dominion-gated call is refused with *Admin access required*.
- **Phase 10's in-lane mutations, as run, each red at its own test**: N1 to N22 on nebula-auth's routes, the refresh and the link spend (N20 after the test was sharpened to a consume that fails before recording), L1 to L7 on the client's logout and the page helper, C18 and C19 on `scope-summary` and the route walk, and F1 on a frame with no injected origin.
- **`session-survives-token-lapse` passed in 931.5 s**, a real wait past the 900 s access-token lifetime with the socket open.
- **Node's `lookup` hook for `*.localhost` is not built.** macOS resolves `*.localhost` to loopback natively, so nothing on this machine can red without it; Phase 14's Linux run measures `ENOTFOUND` first, as its criterion says, and the hook lands there if Linux needs it. Chromium's `--host-resolver-rules` is in `launchChromium`.
- **Phase 10's `/live` mutations, as run, each red at its own limb.** `refresh-reads-the-host` R1 to R7, R3 once limb 3 called `setStarConfig`; `links-sign-nobody-in` N1a to N5, N1a failing limb 1's wait because the page showed no consent card, and N2 with limb 1 bypassed; `links-sign-in-once` S1a to S5; `invites-land-on-their-host` I1 to I3; `logout-ends-every-host` O1 to O4b, O1 re-specified to end one cookie both ways; `hosts-and-frames` H1 to H8; `dev-tab-frame` D1 to D5; `build-box` B1 to B4; U2, U5 and V8; and T1, red at 961.6 s.
- **Three of those first ran green, and each was the mutation's fault.** H3 bypassed `appSpaFallback`'s `isAppHost` guard, which nothing reaches, since vite's catch-all proxy sends a Star's or persona's host to the Worker first; the guard is deleted, and H3′ removes `isAppHost` from the proxy's `bypass` instead. D4's framed `top.location.assign` is blocked by Chromium before limb 4 reads anything; D4′ lets the framed client fall through to its own logout. B1 reddened at the readback before its limb, so B1′ bypasses the readback.
- **Home's summary answers `held`, the scope and admin bit of each accepted live cookie it read**, and `needsFreshLogin` in `home-logic.ts` marks a row that neither its own cookie nor an admin cookie above it covers, which is the refresh's own pick. `parse-id.ts`'s allow-list names it as a client mirror, and its grep now covers Studio's source.
- **The one-cookie model's leftovers went with it**: the cross-email notice, the summary's `current` flag, and `getScopeSummary`'s `currentSub`, which only set the flag. `ConsumeResult`, which nothing read after Phase 10's consume rework, went too.
- **Home's fast-forward now lands in an account's only app.** `first-app-built`, `studio-chat-reload` and ui-smoke's `loginToStudio` waited for the account page; they wait for Studio now.
- **Home links each action to the page that performs it.** An account's "+ App" opens its page at `?create` and its Delete opens the page; an app's Delete opens its Studio at `?app`. `offersAccountActions` and `offersAppDelete` decide which rows carry them, since `docs/vision/auth.md` § *Home* names the app's Delete too.
- **An app deleted from Studio lands on its account's page when the cookie behind the page's token opens it, and on Home otherwise**, decided by `needsFreshLogin` over the token's own membership, so an app's admin with nothing at the account never meets a login there.
- **The everywhere logout's revocations now carry the logout's id**, and a deletion's carry the deletion's, through `#invalidateRefreshTokensForSub`, whose records had none. Their reason is `logout-everywhere`.
- **Two modals broke Studio's stage chain.** Placed between the universe page's `v-else-if` and the preview's `v-else`, they re-attached the `v-else` to the second modal, so the preview rendered on the universe page and vanished under App settings. Both sit outside the chain now.
- **The account delete found a deadlock, so the Galaxy no longer seeds its scaffold in `onStart`.** Deleting an account holding two cold apps constructed both Galaxies at once, and each seeded the scaffold under `blockConcurrencyWhile`. `@cloudflare/computer` 0.3.2's bundled isomorphic-git serializes index and ref writes through module-level locks keyed by path and ref name, which every Galaxy shares; in two runs one Galaxy stopped inside `git.add` while the other finished, until the runtime reset it at 30 s. Its teardown never ran, so its storage was never wiped, and the browser's call timed out. `Galaxy.#workspace()` seeds on first use instead, so a teardown never touches git. Two apps committing at once on one isolate remains exposed, filed in `tasks/backlog.md` as package feedback with an `experiments/` repro as its next step.
- **Two ui-smoke fixture defects, both found by mutation or by the first run.** The form test passed while the account's list was still loading, when the form opens for an empty account too, so it now waits for the list first. The account-delete test lost its Home once the deleted account left one membership, which Home fast-forwards past, so O holds a third account. The delete helper reports a refusal's message or the address it stopped at, rather than a bare timeout.
- **Phase 11's mutations, as run, each red at its own test or limb.** In-lane, nine on `home-logic.ts` and `view-state.ts`: the fresh-login mark covering by a plain cookie or by `startsWith`, the lone-app fast-forward and its frontier, each of `offersAccountActions`'s four conjuncts, and `?app` dropped from the flags. `/live`, ten: `home-lists-every-profile` H1 to H4, `logout-everywhere` L1 to L3, and `studio-app-settings` S1 to S3, S3 refused by the Registry once the facade's pre-check went. ui-smoke, seven: U1 to U6, and G1, which put the seed back in `onStart` and brought the account delete's timeout back.
- **Phases 10 and 11's closing runs, 2026-10-03.** `drive.ts all` passed 50 of 50 with `HARNESS_EMAIL_PROVIDER=resend`. nebula-auth 461 and 4 skipped; `apps/nebula` 959 and 3 skipped, the nine new `home-logic` tests among them; routing 130; testing 203; chromium 14 and 1 skipped, on a second run, since the first timed out in its global setup waiting for the claim page's consent card; ui-smoke 8 of 8, its two skipped tests retargeted and passing; the doc checker, the type-check, and `audit:do-http`, `audit:mesh-vocab`, `audit:email-isolation`, `audit:urls` and `audit:classes` green. mesh 555 across its non-browser projects; its browser lane's setup timed out waiting for mail, because that lane sends through Cloudflare's Email Sending, whose daily quota ran out on 2026-10-03, so it reruns once the quota resets.
- **The predicates take `VerdictClaims`, the token's `aud` beside its `access`**, so passing `access` alone is a type error, which is how every call site was found: the Gateway's passage check, `requireDominionHere` and `requirePassage`, the subscription row's stored verdict, the org tree's bypass, the Galaxy's wipe bit, the Registry's three checks, the Profile's scoped arm, the facade's four, and `canMintFor`. Both fail closed on an absent `aud` or membership.
- **No token can invite a superuser any more.** Dominion over the platform root needs a host whose scope is the root, and there is none, so the facade refuses a platform-tier invite; its `isPlatformScope` branch went, and the refusal is the parse's. A new superuser comes from the bootstrap address alone, and a superuser cannot impersonate another. Both follow from the host rule's decision row; `invite-facade.test.ts` and `impersonation-mint.test.ts` assert the refusals.
- **An admin impersonates from the subject's own page.** The predicate reads the caller's host, and the `aud` validation holds that host inside the subject's scope, so together they leave only the subject's scope. A plain-subject equality first added to the mint could never fire under them and was deleted; `buildNebulaJwtPayload`'s equality stays as the construction check.
- **Thirteen tests flipped, and each moved to the page production uses**: `confine-org-plane`'s claims had stamped `aud: 'ignored'`; `build-reply` and `nebula-client-preview-ready` asked for a chat turn from the `.dev` page; `child3-post`'s mixed-pair client now holds the chat grant a poster on the `.dev` page needs; `client-gateway-passage-delivery`'s admin moved to the galaxy's page and writes Star A's tree through the mesh; `invite-facade`'s tier coverage moved to the universe's page; and the `authScope`-reading cases in `scope-isolation`, `response-leg-scope-gate`, `profile-do` and `dev-studio` were inverted to the host rule. `universeAdminClient`'s JSDoc says dominion reads the page.
- **The `aud` sweep, 2026-10-03**, classes every hit: the two predicates and their `targetScope` inputs; refusal messages; `projectActingToken`'s record and the mint's construction; a client reading its own token; verification's consistency checks; the Registry's `expandScope`, which lists the page's own scope's children; and the facade's invite eligibility, whose `claims.aud === targetScope` § *Claims the build rests on* names. `parse-id.ts`'s grep returns exactly its five exceptions.
- **`auth-flows.md`'s security-layers diagram still drew trust-on-first-use**, a star id stored on the first call and checked on the rest; it now draws verification, the Gateway's binding, passage and dominion as the code runs them. `api-reference.md` gated admin UI on `access.admin`, which no token carries; it reads `scopeAdmin`.
- **Phase 12's mutations, as run, each red at its own test or limb.** The predicates reading `authScope` reddened the predicate tests, the upward-arm sweep for both holders, and `host-rule` limb 1; verification and the mint each without the plain equality; the Profile's system-profile shortcut deleted, and widened back to every profile; the Registry's create and deletion each reading `authScope`; the facade's deletion pre-check skipped, its create pre-check and its invite eligibility each reading `authScope`; the dominion refusal naming `authScope`; the passage refusal and the platform name each saying `Active-scope mismatch` or sharing the passage wording; and the Gateway's check reading `authScope`. The Registry test first matched with `.rejects`, which tripped over the RPC promise the mutated Registry resolved; it now awaits, so an admission reads `(admitted)`.
- **The criterion's Gateway mutation, "hand the predicate `access`", is a type error now and a refusal at run time**, since `access` carries no `aud`; the equivalent that runs reads the membership's scope as the host's, and reds the sibling-tab case.
- **Phase 12's sweep passed 48 of 51, and the three reds were the host rule working.** `node-invite-roundtrip`, `grant-reveals-denied` and `star-serves-current-ontology` each drove the Galaxy's Apply and source from a tenant Star's page, refused at the chat node's grant; each now asks them from Studio, the galaxy's page, through a second driver, and all three pass.
- **A persona's id is `personaId(star, persona)` in `hosts.ts`**, a version-5 UUID of `acme.crm.dev/manny` in the fixed `PERSONA_NAMESPACE`, `68acba14-ab9b-4cdc-9340-fd6d0b976a4f`. The refresh's persona branch reuses Phase 10's candidates and takes only the first accepted admin one; the exact plain fallback is skipped for a persona. A persona under any Star but `dev` gets the refresh's own 401, worded *A persona page opens only in a dev Star*.
- **The opener record is `nebula-auth.worker.refresh.persona`'s `persona minted`**, naming Manny's `sub`, the Star, the page's `aud`, and the opener's `sub`, `profileId`, scope and `scopeAdmin` from its cookie's record, with no acting token. Re-running Phase 12's `aud` sweep finds one new hit, the record's `aud: hostScope`, classed as a record.
- **`persona-host`'s refusals print the error code, never the body**, since a refusal a mutation turns into a success carries a token, which no log may hold.
- **Phase 13's mutations, as run, each red at its own limb**: the host treated as its Star, the persona falling through to the ordinary pick, the accepted conjunct gone, the `dev` check gone, the opener record dropped, the cookie's name deciding, and a random id.
- **Phase 13's closing runs, 2026-10-03.** nebula-auth 467 and 4 skipped; `apps/nebula` 965 and 2 skipped, with `scope-isolation`'s alarm-delivered case red once in the full run and 41 of 41 alone, the contention case `testing.md` names; `persona-host` green after its logging fix. The registry sweep runs once Phase 15's local half lands, covering both.
- **The deploy preflight reads one list.** `apps/nebula/scripts/required-secrets.mjs` names what a deployment needs by its origin, and an `https` origin adds `CERTIFICATE_API_TOKEN`; `deploy.sh` and `deploy-test.sh` both call it, and its selftest runs in the package `test` script. Mutation: drop the token from the list, and the selftest's `https` deploy passes.
- **`CERTIFICATE_ZONE_ID` is a `wrangler.jsonc` var**, production's `lumenize.dev` zone id, read from the API on 2026-10-03. `test-deploy-config.mjs` makes it its sixth swap, from `TEST_CERTIFICATE_ZONE_ID` in `.dev.vars`, and refuses without one; the test zone's token lives there as `TEST_CERTIFICATE_API_TOKEN`. Both names are in `.dev.vars.example`, and neither exists yet: the test zone is Larry's to set up.
- **The wait is `HostWait`, over `host-wait.ts`'s poll.** It renders nothing until the first probe misses, so a host that answers is entered at once, and locally that is every host. Every move into a scope's host waits: the universe page's create and its click into an app, the link page's Accept, and Home's Accept, fast-forward and row click. A click into a listed app waits too, since an app created minutes ago may still lack its certificate; a host that answers costs one probe. Home's Accept goes where the re-read summary would fast-forward a fresh visit (`afterAcceptTarget`), so a ticket-backed signup accepted there lands in its new app; the first cut sent it to the account's page, which rides Universal SSL, and so waited for nothing, which the verifier panel found. The poll's limbs are in `test/frontend/host-wait.test.ts`, each red under its own mutation; the wait itself is a deployed criterion.
- **The first cut of the wait reddened eight scenarios in the sweep, each at a 30 s `waitForURL`.** The link page's answer is sometimes a path, `/` or `/auth/signup`, and `new URL` with no base threw, while every unit fixture had been an absolute URL. A relative URL names the page's own host, which is answering, so it is entered at once, and a fifth test holds that.
- **The stale-pack sweep is `harness/lib/test-scopes.ts`**, run once per deployed run by `drive.ts`, which warns and goes on when `.dev.vars` has no test-zone token. A pack's age is its newest `test-` label inside the zone, so a galaxy minted today under last week's universe is today's. The first run of its zone mutation stayed green, because chopping the test zone's length off a `lumenize.dev` host cut the label to `test-0901`, which no longer parses; the fixture now shares the zone's name as a bare suffix, and the mutation reds.
- **`certificate-wake` watches the wake by operation id.** `provisionAndLogin` accepts on the link's page and then again on Home, so every claim logs two acceptances and wakes its galaxy twice, and the scenario reads each re-accept after the previous one's completion line. Its limb 4 counts a wake as stray when no create's or acceptance's completion line carries its id, which caught the `onStart` mutation's seven wakes, none of them named.
- **A refused acceptance orders nothing by its type.** The Registry's `refused` answer carries no galaxies, so the criterion's mutation, *wake from the helper whatever the outcome*, wakes nothing on its own; it reds only with the refusal also naming its galaxies, and both edits are what `galaxy-cap` limb 4 ran against.
- **Phase 15's mutations, as run, each red at its own limb.** `certificate-wake`: no identity stamped on the `@rawRpc()` entry, and limb 1's marker named no instance; the hook fired only from the link page, and limb 2 found nothing; no wake at the facade's create, limb 3; an order from `onStart`, limb 4; the re-accept's galaxies taken from the spent link's `returnTo`, limb 5; a local Galaxy ordering, limb 6. `galaxy-cap`: the facade waking whatever the Registry answered, and the two-edit acceptance mutation above, both limb 4. The six in-lane sweep mutations and three deploy-config mutations each red their own test.
- **Not built: the deployed venue's names and shared scopes.** Scenarios still claim universes of their own under names of their own: 44 of the 53 registered do, at 74 call sites on 2026-10-03, some inside loops, and `galaxy-cap` adds 19 facade creates. No scenario mints a `test-` name yet, since `testSlug` has no caller but its test, so the stale-pack sweep would reclaim none of what a deployed run orders. Since Phase 8 each claim writes a first app, so a deployed sweep would order a pack per claim, and § *Certificates and the wait*'s clause that a scenario of its own "costs a pack only if it also creates a galaxy" no longer holds. Which scenarios share, and what they share, is Larry's call before Phase 16.
- **Phase 15's closing runs, 2026-10-03, after its last source edit.** `apps/nebula` 995 and 2 skipped, both selftests among its steps: 997 in all against Phase 13's 968, the 29 tests in its four new files. nebula-auth 470 and 4 skipped, the three reap tests. ui-smoke 8 of 8; chromium 14 and 1 skipped, both on `HARNESS_EMAIL_PROVIDER=resend`. `drive.ts all` passed 45 of 53, and the eight reds were the relative-redirect defect above, each green on its re-run after the fix; the full sweep runs again after the verifier panel. The type-check, `audit:do-http`, `audit:mesh-vocab`, `audit:email-isolation`, `audit:urls` and `audit:classes` are green.
- **The verifier panel, 2026-10-03, on Phases 1–13 and 15**: eight readers over grouped phases, one blocker, eleven majors and about fifty minors, nearly all of the minors sentences the build had made false. Every blocker and major is fixed below, each with its mutation as run.
- **The blocker was in mesh: an alarm re-armed under its own id, from its own handler, was deleted when the run ended**, since `triggerAlarms` deleted the fired row by id afterwards. So a certificate pack was ordered once, polled never and retried never, behind green tests that called `certificateAlarm()` directly. `Alarms` now notes a `schedule` of the firing id during the run and keeps that row; a `schedule` from another call while the handler awaits counts too. Deleting before the run was rejected, since it turns an eviction mid-handler from a retry into a loss. Two mesh tests red without it, and the Galaxy's tests now fire the alarm through `triggerAlarms`: two wakes order once with no API call from the wake, a pending pack re-arms and is polled until active, a transient failure retries. Mutations as run: the mesh fix undone, the wake's arm dropped, an order from the wake directly, the alarm's re-arm dropped, each red.
- **A final status now stops the poll.** A pack that timed out or was deleted was polled every ten seconds for good; only the statuses Cloudflare is still working on re-arm, and one ending anywhere but `active` logs at error. Mutation: only `active` final, and the new test reds.
- **The reap's facade half had no test**, since nebula-auth's lane holds no facade. The baseline lane's `GalaxyTest` now runs the fake's `onWake` as the wake arrives, which deletes the galaxy's `Scopes` row; a facade create then logs the reap's `deletion` teardown under its own operation. Mutation: the facade wakes without the re-read, and it reds; the positive control, with the row standing, logs none.
- **A revoke racing a writer's put stranded the record.** The revoke's input gate stood open across its KV deletes, so a put landing after them, checked before the un-index, kept itself, and the un-index then left it live and unindexed. The deletes now run again after the un-index. The race is driven through `registryKvHook`, a wrapper on the test Registry's KV whose hook plays the writer inside the revoke's own delete; it reddened before the fix, its fixture guard showing the writer's check saw the row. `#revokeByHashes`'s JSDoc no longer claims offboarding removes a membership before revoking, which no code does.
- **Majors in the scenarios, each a limb that could not fail on its own:** `galaxy-cap` counted the refused create's teardown markers before any later line, so limb 4 now counts them after the last refusal's completion line, with the refused acceptances' (mutation: teardown before the Registry answers); `scope-teardown` limb 6 had the same shape and an off-by-one, so limb 7 counts it, as the fourth create on that name; `dev-tab-frame` limb 1 read whichever error frame came first, and now reads each iframe by name (mutation: Studio framable, Home not, reds at Studio's assertion); limb 3 posted from Studio's own window, which both checks refuse, and now posts from a second dev-tab frame (mutation: drop the source check) and from Studio's frame navigated to a `data:` page (mutation: drop the origin check), each red at its own assertion; `logout-ends-every-host` limb 4's unparseable cookie sorted past the bound wherever it sat, and now has its own small request; `home-lists-every-profile` limb 3 listened for sockets on `request`, which a WebSocket handshake never fires.
- **The Bearer route walk now reaches every handler.** It sent a scope host's `Origin` with no `Sec-Fetch-Site`, so `sameOriginGuard` answered 403 first; it now sends what a platform-host page sends, and counts the guard's refusals as zero. Mutation: a table row that answers the token's subject, guarded like its neighbours, and it reds.
- **A GET of a link through the Worker writes nothing**, now witnessed in nebula-auth's lane, since vite answers that row locally and no scenario reaches it. Mutation: the GET row consumes, and it reds.
- **The Linux lookup is built.** In `node:24-slim`, `fetch` and `WebSocket` to a `*.lumenize.localhost` host failed with `ENOTFOUND` and connected once `dns.lookup` answered loopback for that suffix, so `harness/lib/localhost-lookup.ts` does that, installed by `drive.ts` and `vitest.config.js`; the chromium lane's provider gains the `--host-resolver-rules` `launchChromium` already passed. Its test stubs the real resolver, since macOS's answers loopback anyway (mutations: no hook, a bare suffix match). Phase 14's run on `ubuntu-latest` is its standing witness.
- **The vite-driven scenarios can now run deployed in shape**: `bootStudioVite` boots nothing under `HARNESS_TARGET_URL` and hands back the deployment's hosts, and `signUpInBrowser` derives the app's host from the venue and waits up to five minutes there, for the count-up. Scenarios that log a superuser in through `bootVars.NEBULA_AUTH_BOOTSTRAP_EMAIL` still cannot run deployed, since a deployed target's bootstrap address is its own secret; that is Phase 16's to settle with the shared scopes.
- **The super-admin profile check has a `/live` witness**: `superuser-end-to-end` limb 7 writes a stranger's profile from a page beside them and is refused by message, limb 6 from a page above them as its positive control. Mutation: the platform short-circuit on every profile, and limb 7 reds.
- **Smaller fixes from the panel**: `deploy.sh`'s preflight calls the fail-closed CLI, since a failed list in its `for` ran zero times; the cookie jar ignores an empty `Domain` and compares it case-insensitively (a mutation each); Home's collapsed rows carry the app delete; `audit:urls` now reads `location`'s host, origin, hostname, protocol and port; `impersonate-lifetime`'s fixture guard, which never ran, runs in a universe of its own and revokes by the platform host's logout; the browser test worker's `Env` is regenerated. The false sentences are corrected: the root-admin seed in tests and in the published `coding-your-ui.md`, the one-admin-per-universe claims, `authScope` deciding where `aud` now does, the facade header, the Registry's deletion JSDocs, the broadcast default in mesh's comments, `auth-flows.md`'s refresh and same-origin rule, and the Studio UI README and `dev-studio.sh`, which still described the login before this build.
- **What the panel found that stays as it is, recorded:** no public surface carries `impersonation_ended`, since a child never receives `onLoginRequired` by design, so `impersonation-lifecycle` limb 7 asserts the child ends and leaves its parent; `findRawRpcMethod` reads a method's value, so a getter or a dotted path can never qualify and the raw-RPC tests for them cannot red under "drop the decorator check"; the route grep is a `git grep`, blind to the build's untracked files until they are committed; `unplugin-swc` was added to nebula-auth's devDependencies in an earlier session with no approval recorded, for Larry; and `testing.md`, `mesh.md` and `coding-style.md` stay over the prose budget they were already over.
- **A first plain run of `dev-tab-frame` failed during the mutation batch, unexplained**, its log showing only the teardowns' `scope-deleted` lines and a lost network connection; the next plain run passed all five limbs, and so did every later one.
- **The closing runs after the panel's fixes, 2026-10-03.** `apps/nebula` 1004 and 2 skipped, against 997 before the panel; the nine new tests are the three Galaxy alarm tests, the two facade reap tests, the after-Accept pair, the relative-URL wait and the lookup test, less `impersonate-lifetime`'s guard, which now runs inside its existing test. nebula-auth 472 and 4 skipped, the race and the link GET; mesh 557 across its non-browser projects, the two re-arm tests; `testing` 205, the two Domain tests; ui-smoke 8 of 8; chromium 14 and 1 skipped. `drive.ts all` passed 52 of 53: `home-lists-every-profile`'s new socket listener counted vite's hot-reload socket, so it counts only the Gateway's, passes on its re-run, and reds when Home opens a Gateway socket. The type-check, the doc checker, `platform-embed` and every audit are green. `impersonate-lifetime`'s guard, running for the first time, failed until its connect was made to refresh: the client still held a live token, so the clock moves past it; dropping the helper's `extraConfig` now reds it.
- **The SQLite inside a Durable Object refuses a `LIKE` pattern past 50 bytes, and a run's test scopes passed it.** `impersonation-lifecycle`, moved onto the shared app, failed in `executeScopeDeletion` with `LIKE or GLOB pattern too complex`: the cascade matched `${target}.%`, and three `test-…` labels make 60 bytes. A galaxy named at the slug limit makes 62, so production would have met it on any long name. Every pattern built from a scope is now `parse-id.ts`'s `descendantRange`, the range from `acme.crm.` up to `acme.crm/`, exact for any string and needing no escaping: `#scopesAtOrBeneath` serves a deletion, an acceptance's teardown and claim convergence, and `#levelClause` the summary's levels. Three nebula-auth tests at the slug limit red under the `LIKE` form with the cap's own message, one per read. The deletion's prefix-sibling test now uses `del-1-2`, since `del-1x` sorts past the range and stayed green with the range's dot dropped. `#galaxiesAtOrBeneath` lost its platform-root arm, which no mutation could red because no caller passes the root. nebula-auth 475 and 4 skipped, up three, and `impersonation-lifecycle` passes.
- **The scenarios moved onto the shared app, 2026-10-03.** Every scenario that claims an account names it with `testSlug`, `four-party-chat`'s fixed `claude-four` and `studio-chat-reload`'s `claude-browser` among them. Three more scenarios share the run's app, `grant-reveals-denied`, `node-invite-roundtrip` and `studio-signed-out-landing`, beside the fifteen before them. The rest each leave members, settings or org-tree nodes in the app they run in, or have the app itself as their subject, so they keep accounts of their own. `signUpInBrowser`, and the two raw signups a scenario accepts, now push onto `claimedUniverses`, which had missed them; a claim never accepted orders no pack and stays off it. `inviteViaMesh` waits for its host, and every arrival behind a count-up waits `NEW_HOST_TIMEOUT_MS`, six minutes on the deployed target. `studio-signed-out-landing` spelled the apex from the local origin, and `studio-overlays-by-url` visited an app before it existed, which has no certificate deployed; both changed.
- **The deployed target ordered no pack: mesh's alarms never armed a job due now.** `Alarms.#scheduleNextAlarm` pointed the Durable Object's alarm only at jobs later than now, and `schedule(0, …)` stores the current second, so the Galaxy's wake recorded its job and nothing fired it until the deletion happened to arm the alarm, 126 s later in the tail. A `Date` already past was stranded the same way. The filter came with the `cloudflare/actors` code `alarms.ts` was adapted from; the `agents` SDK's scheduler, its successor, arms the earliest job clamped to just after now, and mesh now does the same.
- **No running system had exercised that alarm**: every in-lane test fired its job by hand, and a local stack's `http` origin never schedules the order. Two mesh tests now wait for the runtime to deliver a zero delay and a past `Date` (mutation: the old filter, and both red), and the Galaxy's three alarm tests wait for that delivery rather than firing the first step themselves; the two mesh tests that scheduled jobs in the past to fire by hand now schedule them a minute out. Larry chose to fix it here and not upstream, and to backlog rebasing on the `agents` scheduler (`tasks/backlog.md` § *Lumenize Mesh*). mesh's main project 536, `apps/nebula`'s certificate file 12.
- **Phase 14's Linux run found three things, 2026-10-03.** ui-smoke's Chromium was refused at `platform.lumenize.localhost:5174`: in `node:24-slim`, Node resolves `localhost` to `::1` first, so a vite bound to `localhost` listens on IPv6 alone, while `--host-resolver-rules` maps the hosts to 127.0.0.1. `bootStudioVite` now binds and proxies on 127.0.0.1. CI's check that the committed `worker-configuration.d.ts` files are current had never run before, since CI last ran in June, and could never pass: `wrangler types` declares every key of the `.dev.vars` it reads, and CI writes a different `.dev.vars` from any developer's. `generate-types.sh` now passes `--env-file .dev.vars.example`, which overrides `.dev.vars`, so every machine generates the same `Env`; the repo type-checks on it. `run-live-scenarios` stopped on Cloudflare's daily sending quota, which the day's runs had spent.
- **`deleted-on-cloudflare` reads a deletion's effects from Cloudflare's own API.** Packs through the zone's certificate API; storage through the account's Durable Object listing, which trails the objects by six to eight minutes (watched for eight minutes on 2026-10-03), so the scenario learns the app's ids from `wrangler tail`, whose events carry `durableObjectId`, and waits for the listing both ways. The sweep on a deployed target reports the zone's app packs after each scenario, their peak, and every pack it leaves, named.
- **`signup-to-first-app` gains limb 13**, a ticket-backed signup accepted on Home, in a browser holding only that address: Home opens the consent itself and fast-forwards into the app, behind the count-up on the deployed target. No scenario rendered that path; limb 12 stops at the consent. Mutation: Home stays put after the Accept, and limb 13 reds locally. The limb 3 and app-create count-up checks run on the deployed target only, where the host has no certificate yet.
- **`deleted-on-cloudflare`'s limbs 1 to 4 pass on the deployed target, 2026-10-03**: no pack left by an immediate or a racing delete, the first app's and the Universal pack kept, and the deleted app's Galaxy listed holding data before the delete and none after, with each object's teardown reading the abort's rejection as the reset it ordered. Limb 5 first failed on a pack Cloudflare still listed after the account's delete: an active pack, once deleted, is listed as `pending_deletion` for ten minutes and more, so the scenario and the sweep's count treat that status as gone. An earlier run lost limb 5 to the harness instead: the driver had been handed its token, so its cookie jar held nothing to renew from, and its first call after the token's fifteen minutes timed out. `connectDriver`'s JSDoc now says so, and the scenario's driver signs in itself.
- **The four deployed mutations, 2026-10-03.** Skipping the pack delete reds limb 1, dropping `deleteAll()` reds limb 4, and counting the abort's rejection a failure reds limb 4 too, whose tail never shows the ordered reset. On the clean redeploy all five limbs pass, in 1032 s. **Dropping the macrotask yield before `ctx.abort()` did not red limb 4**: an awaited `deleteAll()` followed straight by the abort left no stored data on Cloudflare. `durable-objects.md` § *Persist before `ctx.abort()`* measured a synchronous `kv.put` lost without the yield, which this does not contradict, so the yield may be unneeded after an awaited `deleteAll()`; one run decides nothing, and it stays until Larry weighs in. Mutation A's account kept its three packs, since its build skipped the delete; they were deleted by hand.
- **Phase 16's guidance, 2026-10-03.** Every row § *Backlog rows this build settles* names now carries its verdict in `backlog.md`: the rate-limit row narrowed to the connection limiter, with a per-`sub` Gateway row beside it; the Gateway-`aud` and accessor rows closed, the second folded into § *Decisions*' row on `access`; the `createNebulaClient` auto-detect row deleted; both impersonation rows and the mesh-bridge row corrected to `NebulaAuthFacade.impersonate`; the self-narrowing row answered for the platform half and left open for the other; the capacity watch given the orphaned-pack sweep and the owner cap; the reaper relieved of pack deletion; the Safari row given today's cookies and the certificate's label depth; the `/gateway/` row given the passage check.
- **Phase 16's docs and greps, 2026-10-03.** `auth-flows.md` shows the certificate order and the count-up, `packages/nebula-auth/README.md`'s acceptance and claim rows say what they now do, and `platform-embed.ts` is regenerated. The triage grep's two stale hits, ADR-012's renamed test and ADR-021's "is today", are fixed. The old host is named only where its retirement is, after `RELEASING.md`, `local-config.mjs`, `wrangler.jsonc`'s warning and the Turnstile test's URLs. Every pointer into this file resolves. `nebula-pre-alpha.md`'s line on the reaper now says teardown deletes the pack, and its wipe seeds both superusers.
- **The deployed sweep's first attempt, 2026-10-03, stopped after three scenarios to fix what it found.** `message-roundtrip` failed in both venues: its last limb opens a second tab that refreshes from the driver's cookie jar, and the shared-app conversion had handed the driver a token, leaving the jar empty, so the tab's refresh answered 401 and it never subscribed. Its driver now signs in as the shared owner through `connectDriver`'s own `email`. `superuser-end-to-end` limb 5 loaded a page on a four-deep host, which no certificate covers deployed; the page load runs locally only, and the refresh half, an `Origin` on the platform host, runs in both. And the sweep killed every `workerd` before each scenario even against a deployed target, which took down a vitest run beside it; it now does so only for a local sweep.
- **Larry approved, 2026-10-03:** `unplugin-swc` in nebula-auth's devDependencies, added in an earlier session without a recorded approval; and one sentence in `calibration.md` §13 naming the alarm case, in place of a new `testing.md` rule. The proposed `/build-task` edit, to sweep the task file's own design intent for text a phase falsified, was dropped as one occurrence.
- **The verifier panel over Phases 14–16, 2026-10-03, and what it changed.** `generate-types.sh` wrote its absolute `--env-file` path into every header, so CI's diff could never pass; it passes the path relative to each config, and the header reads `../../.dev.vars.example` everywhere. The lookup hook never reached vitest's test workers, which do not load the config, so `browser`, `ui-smoke` and `browser-bench` install it from a setup file, and Chromium maps the bare apex too. A Linux runner with systemd-resolved, such as `ubuntu-latest`, answers `*.localhost` itself, so the hook matters only on a Linux without one, and no CI run can witness it; the JSDoc, the test header and § *Design intent* now say so. `.claude/skills/live/SKILL.md` needed nothing.
- **What the panel changed in `deleted-on-cloudflare` and the sweep.** Limb 3's control and limb 5's Universal check count only live packs, since a wrongly deleted one stays listed `pending_deletion`; limb 2 asserts its raced create landed; limb 4 learns the Galaxy's id only from the Galaxy's own teardown line, and checks the Galaxy alone, because a `.dev` Star holds nothing to be listed with. The sweep fails when every scenario passed yet a pack it added names anything but the run's shared app. Cleanup runs only against a deployed target, and a failed refresh is no longer reported as a claim never accepted. `wakeCertificates` logs a failed re-read and never rejects (mutation: no guard, and its new test reds), and `CERTIFICATE_API_TOKEN` is named in `.dev.vars.example`, so the generated `Env` types it and `galaxy.ts` no longer widens `Env`.
- **The full deployed sweep, 2026-10-03, ran 53 of 54 before the two-hour limit stopped it during `deleted-on-cloudflare`, which had passed alone.** Twelve failed, none in product code; each was the harness assuming the local venue. Cloudflare injects a `/cdn-cgi/rum` beacon on the test zone, which three request captures counted as ours; a deployed Worker answers over HTTP/2, so Chrome prints `401 ()` where HTTP/1.1 prints `401 (Unauthorized)`; `build-box` probed with `node:http`; `turnstile-canary` needs a secret the test target lacks, as production does; the persistent deployed superuser has its platform membership accepted after the first run, which three scenarios assumed pending; `hosts-and-frames` planted a cookie on the local domain and named pages by vite's source entries rather than their titles; three scenarios armed a 120 s email waiter before an invite that first waits out a new app's certificate; `star-serves-current-ontology` gave that wait a 45 s step; and a deleted app's host has no certificate, so `scope-teardown`'s watcher cannot reach the wiped Galaxy, which a deployed run now reports as not observable.
- **Accepted residuals from the panel.** A pack that ends in a final status other than `active` leaves `HostWait` counting with no bound, the Galaxy's error log the only signal. Every teardown lists the zone's packs, one API call per 50 galaxies, a cost that grows with the zone and that the capacity watch row covers. The deployed superuser is one address, so a cleanup letter arriving late could meet a later scenario's waiter for it. And `grant-reveals-denied` and `node-invite-roundtrip` apply the seed ontology to the shared app, a no-op after the run's first.
- **Closing runs after the panel round, 2026-10-03.** nebula-auth 476 and 4 skipped, one more for the re-read test; mesh's main and alarms projects 537; `apps/nebula` 1007 and 2 skipped; the local sweep 54 of 54, Docker scenarios included. mesh's browser lane waits on Cloudflare's daily sending quota, which the day's runs spent.
- **The second set of deployed mutations, 2026-10-03, each red in its own scenario.**
  - A count-up that never waits reds `signup-to-first-app` at limb 3.
  - A Galaxy that orders no pack reds `studio-overlays-by-url` at its six-minute wait for the app's host.
  - The old `run_worker_first` list reds `build-box`'s `no-store` check.
  - Dropping `frame-ancestors` reds `hosts-and-frames` at limb 4.
  - A teardown deleting every pack that names a host reds `deleted-on-cloudflare` at its first delete, where the first app's pack must still be listed.
  - Removing `connectDriver`'s host wait, a harness change needing no deploy, reds `scope-teardown`, whose client never connects.
  - The page-path and Upgrade-refusal mutations were not run deployed: the Worker code they change is the same in both venues, and Phase 10 mutation-checked both locally.
- **The teardown mutation took the test zone's TLS down, as it had to.** Limb 3's control is that the zone's Universal pack survives, so the mutation deleted that pack, and every host on `lumenize-test.dev` failed its handshake. Cloudflare did not reissue it on its own. With Larry's approval, Universal SSL was turned off and on again through the zone's API, the recovery Cloudflare's troubleshooting page gives, and a new pack was active about two minutes later. Running that mutation again needs the same recovery after it.
- **Test mail moved to Resend in every lane but one, 2026-10-03.** CI on this build failed twice on `account daily sending quota exceeded`, from the `send_email` binding.
  - **The cause.** A binding send counts against Cloudflare's daily quota unless its recipient is a verified destination address. A catch-all that delivers to a Worker verifies nothing. A sender on a routing-only domain may reach only verified addresses, and the binding refused it for a per-run `lumenize-test.dev` address, then accepted it for `claude@lumenize.io` once Larry had verified that address (`experiments/email-quota-probe/RESULTS.md`).
  - **The change.** `apps/nebula/wrangler.jsonc` sets `EMAIL_PROVIDER: "resend"` and declares no binding, so the deploy scripts' `--var` and the harness's `HARNESS_EMAIL_PROVIDER` are gone. `packages/auth`'s Hono lane and mesh's browser lane moved too, and their mail now carries an SES message id where the binding's ended `@lumenize.io`.
  - **What stays.** `packages/auth`'s `e2e-email` lane keeps the binding, as the Cloudflare transport's one real-send test at a few sends a run. Larry is filing Cloudflare's limit-increase request.
- **The final deployed sweep, 2026-10-03, passed 53 of 54 on `04bf150`, and the 54th passes since its fix.** The run left one pack, its shared app's.
  - **What failed.** `superuser-end-to-end` limb 4 assumed Home's summary reached the probe's app. The summary fills level by level within a 50-row budget, and the target's ~25 accounts spent it on other universes' apps first.
  - **The fix.** The limb asserts the universe on the summary, which is what its platform-arm mutation reds, and lists the app with the facade's `expandScope` from the universe's page, as Home does, in both venues. Mutation: let `expandScope` answer an empty level for a platform membership, and the limb reds on the app.
  - **What it leaves.** The universe level holds the remaining exposure, and it fills once the target holds about 50 accounts.
